RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY 
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
Office of City Clerk 
 
May 24, 2021 
 
 
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning 
at 6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers and via electronic conference meeting.  Present were: MAYOR 
– Patrick Collins, COUNCIL MEMBERS – Dr. Michelle Aldrich, Bryan Cook, Richard Johnson, 
Pete Laybourn, Dr. Mark Rinne, Scott Roybal, Tom Segrave and Jeff White. Absent: Ken 
Esquibel.  Also present: Kris Jones, City Clerk.  The pledge of allegiance was recited. 
 
 
EXPRESSION OF APPRECIATION TO THE 2020-2021 MAYOR’S YOUTH 
COUNCIL AND STATE OF THE YOUTH ADDRESS. Jan Spires, Youth Alternatives and staff 
advisor to the Mayor’s Youth Council, expressed appreciation to Youth Council members, 
recognized their year in service and introduced Emily Lucero, Chair, for the Mayor’s Youth 
Council, who presented the State of the Youth Address.  Ms. Lucero reviewed projects and 
programs the group participated in throughout the year.  She expressed appreciation to Ms. Spires, 
Michael Skinner, City Public Information Officer, Haylee Chenchar and Amber Ash with the 
Downtown Development Authority, Mayor Collins and fellow Youth Council members.  Ms. 
Spires thanked the member’s parents for allowing them to participate with the council and 
introduced and presented awards to the following members: Kaylee Ashley, David Barker, Ty 
Bronder, Garrison Cook, Makiya Green, Nevaeh Green, Emily Lucero, Zoey Lundin and Elizabeth 
Stump.  
 
 
Consent Agenda.  (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA] are considered to 
be routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no 
separate discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support 
by two other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered 
in its normal sequence on the agenda).  Mr. Johnson declared a conflict of interest due to an 
employment affiliation and did not vote.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve, seconded by Mr. White.  
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of 
Mr. Johnson who did not vote. 
 
[CA] 
Minutes from Regular Meeting of the Governing Body on May 10, 2021.  Motion (per 
Consent Agenda) carried. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a retail liquor license transfer of location 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for Poor Richard’s, from 2233 E. Lincolnway to 
1808 O’Neil Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Inquiry was made related to the location and scope of 
the business and it was noted no representatives were present to report on the inquiry.  There being 
no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed 
the hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 

   
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a retail liquor license transfer of ownership 
application filed with the City Clerk’s Office for ARCHCO Albany, LLC d/b/a Albany Restaurant, 
Bar, and Package Liquor, 1506 Capitol Ave., Cheyenne, Wyoming (license currently held by 
Albany, Inc. d/b/a Albany Restaurant, Bar and Liquormart).  Brian Bau, ARCHCO Albany, LLC 
representative, advised he was available to answer any questions.  There being no further 
comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Collins closed the hearing 
and referred to application to Finance Committee. 
 
 
Mayor Collins opened a public hearing for a bar and grill liquor license application filed 
with the City Clerk’s Office for Capitol Cuisine LLC d/b/a Ike’s Bar & Grill, 122 West 6th St., 
Cheyenne, Wyoming. Upon inquiry, Brian Eicholtz, Ike’s Bar & Grill, provided information on 
the extensive remodel of the building and advised the business will be a neighborhood bar and 
grill.  There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, 
Mayor Collins closed the hearing and referred the application to Finance Committee. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the City of Cheyenne’s Solid Waste Fee 
Schedule.  Vicki Nemecek, Public Works Director, advised she was available for any questions.  
Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, 
seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present with the exception of Mr. Cook voting “no”. (#4361) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Amending the Unified Development Code (UDC) to 
increase the total lot coverage of the MD1, MD2, P-CV1, P-CV2, and P-CV3 lot types to 80%.    
Dr. Rinne advised there was no recommendation on the item at the Public Services Committee.  
Dr. Rinne moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Comments 
were made regarding continued lack of maintenance of landscaping following planting resulting 
in death of plants/trees which are not being replaced.  Discussion included the need to review 
landscaping requirements and maintenance responsibilities.  It was noted that the proposed 
amendment only effects certain lot types and is a way to save water usage.  Concern was raised 
regarding reduced area of green in some areas to 50’ x 50’, noting landscaping is important for 
various reasons.  Dr. Rinne moved to amend all references to 80% be changed to 70%, seconded 
by Dr. Aldrich.  Dr. Rinne noted the proposed amendment is an attempt to compromise, and his 
preference would be to revisit in a year.  It was reported that some lots currently have less than the 
70% required.  Motion to amend failed. Voting “yes” - Dr. Aldrich, Mr. Laybourn, Dr. Rinne and 
Mr. White.  Voting “no” - Mayor Collins, Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Mr. Roybal and Mr. Segrave. 
Main motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with exception 
of Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#4362)  
 
 
ORDINANCE –3rd READING – Creating Chapter 8.29, Hood System Cleaning 
Companies, of Title 8, Health and Safety, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Upon inquiry, 
Division Chief Byron Mathews, Cheyenne Fire & Rescue Department, clarified that businesses 
will be required to be licensed moving forward with no provisions to grandfather businesses.  Chief 
Mathews explained that although there may be an initial impact on businesses due to uniformity 
and initial higher cost for cleaning, eventually there would be a cost savings which will result in 

   
an overall safety for consumers moving forward.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present. (#4363) 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending Section 5.12.030, Licenses and Permits-
Types, of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and 
Regulations, of the Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on 
second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present.   
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming.  Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
White. It was noted that initially it was believed delivery services may reduce DUI’s, however, 
after the limited 6-month period during the COVID pandemic, they actually increased. Further  
comments included: support for the ordinance, limited time frame and increased alcohol intake 
due to the COVID pandemic may have contributed to the increase of DUI’s, more establishments 
will likely participate if delivery is available permanently and the importance of businesses being 
able to compete and provide additional services.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of 
the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn and Dr. Rinne voting no. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Amending various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic 
Liquor and Malt Beverages, of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code of the City 
of Cheyenne, Wyoming Mr. Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. 
Segrave.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Repealing Chapter 2.52, Personnel Commission, and 
creating a new Chapter 2.52, Personnel System, of Title 2, Administration and Personnel, of the 
Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. Seth Lloyd, Planning and Development Department and 
CPEA representative, noted concerns relating to this and the following agenda items pertaining to 
proposed changes to the Personnel Commission.  Mr. Lloyd reported dissolving the commission 
is concerning to city employees and requested clarification on the effect this will have on 
employees, urging caution proceeding with the proposed changes.  Linda Burt, Personnel 
Commission Vice Chair, voiced concerns and explained members of the commission are 
volunteers and the commission provides fairness and due process.  Ms. Burt noted the proposal 
calls for the hearing officer to be paid for by the city which may give the appearance of bias.  Dr. 
Rinne moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.   Dr. Rinne moved to amend 
by substitute dated May 24, 2021, seconded by Mr. White.  City Attorney Mike O’Donnell 
explained the amendment clarifies that the existing grievance procedure will not be eliminated.  
Mayor Collins overruled a point of order raised regarding comments not being germane to the 
agenda item on the floor.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins advised concerns were raised on removing 
the opportunity of having a jury of peers participate in hearing procedures for city employees.  Mr. 
Lloyd reported there is an impression by city employees that the commission is a trial by jury and 
if the city appoints a hearing officer, that person may be predisposed to rule in favor of the city.  It 
was additionally noted employees fear the new procedure won’t be as fair as the current process. 
Upon inquiry, Mr. O’Donnell provided information on the current Personnel Commission which 

   
consists of 7 members - 3 city employee representatives and 4 citizens appointed by the Mayor.  
Mr. O’Donnell explained that due to the volunteer status of members, it can be difficult to assemble 
the commission for hearings.  Motion to amend carried.  Comments followed relating to support 
for the ordinance, informal grievance process, concerns involving one individual to serve as 
hearing officer, establishing a fair process, inclusion of a neutral party hearing officer, providing 
training to Personnel Commission members and potential of a conflict of interest when employee 
representative members of the commission are employees of the same department the petitioner is 
employed by.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.

ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Approving and establishing revised water and sewer 
rates, tap and system development fees, administrative fees, service charges and penalties, and 
septic and commercial waste dump rates, as recommended by the Board of Public Utilities.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. White.  Comments were made 
acknowledging that fee increases aren’t generally favored and that BOPU did an excellent job of 
looking at surrounding communities’ fee structures as references for the fee adjustments.  Motion 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
 
 
ORDINANCE –2nd READING – Appropriating moneys for the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming for various purposes in conducting the municipal government of said City and fixing 
the amount of general and special taxes as part of the revenue required to meet the said 
appropriation, all for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 2021 and ending June 30, 2022.  Mr. White 
moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Mr. White moved to amend by 
substitute dated May 20, 2021 including the following amendments: increasing the City Attorney 
Division by $123,200.00 and decreasing Miscellaneous Division by the same amount; increasing 
the Clean and Safe Division by $73,000.00 and decreasing the Miscellaneous Division by the same 
amount and increasing the Compliance Division by $18,278.00 and increase revenue from reserves 
in the same amount, seconded by Mr. Segrave.  Dr. Rinne moved to divide the question to discuss 
and vote on the amendments separately, seconded by Mr. Johnson.  Motion carried.   Voting “yes” 
– all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Roybal and Mr. White 
voting “no”.  Item #1-motion to approve increasing the City Attorney Division by $123,200.00 
and decreasing Miscellaneous Division by the same amount carried.  Voting “yes” – all members 
of the governing body present.  Item #2-increasing the Clean and Safe Division by $73,000.00 and 
decreasing the Miscellaneous Division by the same and amount.  Discussion included:  Use of 
one-time funds for on-going expenses such as permanent full-time employees, options for other 
funding sources that may be available for Depot Plaza repairs and maintenance, and efforts to fund 
positions from the general fund rather than one-time funding.  Upon inquiry, Robin Lockman, City 
Treasurer, advised depending on sales tax collections, it could be possible to use some 

   
discretionary funds to aid in moving employees under the general fund.  Comments were made 
regarding sales tax funding for the Botanic Gardens operations and maintenance which is currently 
be utilized for a few employees’ salaries and concerns relating to depleting that funding.  Support 
was voiced for moving full-time staff under the general fund instead of temporary tax funding.  
Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins clarified the proposed funding could be allocated to other areas of 
the budget if the motion fails.  Additional discussion included the importance of repairing and 
maintaining the Depot Plaza for tourism and image, noting improvements will help generate sales 
tax and other revenue.  Comments were made on the value of operations and maintenance funding, 
functions of the Clean & Safe Division, and lack of available funding to move Botanic Gardens 
staff under the general fund.   Upon inquiry, Mayor Collins advised a vote in favor of the motion 
before the governing body would move the funding into the Clean & Safe Division.  Ms. Lockman, 
upon inquiry, reported the funding in question would only take care of the salary for one position, 
noting there is still significant amount of benefits to be covered.  Jason Sanchez, Community 
Recreation & Events Deputy Director, explained a recent site visit was done at the Depot Plaza 
and several areas for repair and maintenance were identified and that funds from this amendment 
would be used for that purpose.  Mr. Sanchez reviewed Depot Plaza areas that need maintained 
and noted the goal is to restore the plaza.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with Mr. Cook, Mr. Johnson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. White voting “no”.  Item 
3-increasing the Compliance Division by $18,278.00 and increase revenue from reserves in the 
same amount. Comments were made regarding a recent Ward I Town Hall public meeting which 
highlighted nuisance issues.  Supportive comments were voiced noting the importance of assisting 
in the efforts.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.   Main 
motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body 
present.  The following is a summary of the proposed budget, including the aforementioned 
amendments: General Fund, $53,795,619; Weed and Pest, $573,274; Youth Alternatives Grants, 
$882,886; Recreation Programs, $1,477,947; Community Development Block Grant, $617,074; 
Law Enforcement Grants, $252,616; Transportation Planning, $866,101; Transit, $2,603,121; 
Juvenile Justice, $67,640; Special Friends, $84,664; Development Impact Fees, $212,800; One 
Percent Sales Tax 2019-22, $11,087,381; Specific Purpose Option Tax, $601,905; Youth 
Activities/Ropes, $18,221; Golf Facilities, $276,590; Fleet Maintenance, $4,460,139; Solid Waste 
Management, $19,301,898; Civic Center, $2,057,777; Ice & Events Center, $828,665; Perpetual 
Care, $3,000; Total City, $100,069,318; Water Enterprise, $23,619,580; Sewer Enterprise, 
$14,123,800; Total Appropriation, $137,812,698.

RESOLUTION – Rescinding Resolution No. 6023 which establishes that all revenue from 
City Contract No. 5902 be split evenly between the City of Cheyenne Solid Waste Enterprise Fund, 
the Board of Public Utilities, and the newly established Belvoir Ranch Recreation Special Revenue 
Fund.  Cari Cuffney spoke in favor of the anticipated substitute to be introduced and noted the 
contract provides ongoing maintenance funds for the Belvoir Ranch masterplan development.  Mr. 
Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. White.  Mr. Roybal moved to amend by substitute dated 
May 17, 2021, seconded by Dr. Aldrich.  Mr. O’Donnell provided information on the substitute 
stating the original proposed resolution redirected all money to the general fund and the proposed 
substitute amendment directs 2/3 of the funding to the general fund and 1/3 to the Belvoir Ranch 
Recreation Special Revenue fund.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Johnson voting “no”.  Main motion as amended 
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. 
Johnson voting “no”.  (#6146)   
 
 
RESOLUTION – Authorizing the Downtown Development Authority (DDA) to generate 
revenue for general operating funds.  Amber Ash, Executive Director of DDA, explained that as 
part of the annual strategic planning process and in effort to become financially stable, it was 
determined the DDA would seek approval of a resolution authorizing approval to generate revenue 
utilizing concepts such as advertising options, apparel sales, sponsorships as well as other revenue 
generating efforts.  She explained the DDA has faced challenges relating to unstable revenues over 
the last few years and they wish to look at different ways for sustainability.  Ms. Ash reported at 
the May 17th Finance Committee meeting, a recommendation to postpone the resolution to the next 
meeting of governing body was made.  In response to that, a substitute was drafted which removes 
flexibility for funding options leaving specified options.  Wendy Volk voiced support and 
applauded what the DDA has presented to council.  Mr. Roybal moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. 
White.  Mr. Roybal moved to postpone to June 14, 2021, seconded by Mr. Laybourn.  Dr. Rinne 
advised a substitute amendment was forthcoming and requested the postponement be voted down 
in order to consider the amendment.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “no” all members of the 
governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting “yes”.  Dr. Aldrich moved to 
amend by substitute dated May 24, 2021, seconded by Mr. Roybal.  Mr. O’Donnell provided 
information on the substitute noting it removes the flexibility and only allows for funds to be raised 
for the listed items.  Comments and additional details were provided on the proposed substitute 
amendment.  Concerns were raised relating to allowing full flexibility and the potential appearance 
of competition with merchants located downtown.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all 
members of the governing body present with the exception of Mayor Collins, Mr. Johnson and Dr. 
Rinne voting “no”.  Additional concerns were raised regarding advertising, amount of available 
DDA reserve funding and turnover rate of executive directors and board members during the past 
6 years which contributes to the success of the DDA.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting 
“yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Laybourn voting 
“no”.  (#6147)

ORDINANCE NO.
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING VARIOUS SECTIONS OF CHAPTER
5.12, ALCOHOLIC LIQUOR AND MALT BEVERAGES, OF TITLE 5,
BUSINESS LICENSES AND REGULATIONS, OF THE CODE OF THE
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING.”
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING:
Section 1. That various sections of Chapter 5.12, Alcoholic Liquor and Malt Beverages,
of Title 5, Business Licenses and Regulations, of the Code ofthe City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, are
hereby amended as set out in this ordinance.
5.12.020
Definitions.
“Delivery service” means the taking of orders and providing the transporting and delivery
of alcoholic liquor and malt beverage products to patrons at a residence, organization or business
for a fee, in violation of the provisions of the chapter. and a liquor licensee’s process by which
their employee or contractor provides alcohol to a purchaser.
1.
“Delivery” means the act of providing alcohol to a purchaser.
2.
“Delivery Employee” means the licensee’s employee or contractor responsible for
delivering alcohol to the purchaser.
3.
“Sealed” means in its original package and unopened-factory sealed (bottle of wine or
spirits/case of beer), in a plastic bag, heat sealed closed, or in a container that has a
breakable seal incorporated in the top.
4.
“Serve” means the act of delivering alcohol to a purchaser or delivery employee or
contractor on the licensed premises.
5.12.070
Restrictions upon license or permit applicants and holders.
B.
Retail Liquor Licensees.
3.
A licensee shall not may provide any alcoholic liquor or malt beverage delivery
services in conjunction with the licensee’s business operations pursuant to Wyo. Stat. Section 12-
5-601.
a.
Retail liquor licensees, microbrewery permittees, winery permittees,
satellite winery permittees, and satellite manufacturing permittees, may be permitted to
provide delivery service for the delivery of alcohol to customers.
Microbreweries and
wineries shall only be permitted to provide delivery of their manufactured product.
Delivery services shall be subject to the following:
i.
No order shall be received from nor delivery made to a person under twenty-
one (21) years of age.
Each purchaser shall be required to provide a valid
driver’s license or valid government issued identification upon delivery.
ii.
No part of a publicly owned sidewalk, highway, street, or alley shall be used
for taking orders or conducting sales.
No sales shall be made to intoxicated
individuals pursuant to Section 5.12.160(C) of this chapter.
iii. Alcohol shall only be delivered to customers in a sealed package.
iv. Delivery shall not be made to or on public property and shall not be delivered
outside the city of Cheyenne boundaries unless otherwise allowed by Wyoming
Statute, resolution of the Laramie County Commissioners, or order of the
Governor of Wyoming.
1

v.
Delivery service employees shall have completed an alcohol server training
programs as approved by the Wyoming Liquor Division and pursuant to Section
5.12.090 of this chapter; shall be at least twenty-one (21) years of age; shall
maintain a valid driver’s license; shall maintain valid vehicle insurance
coverage as required by Wyoming Statute; shall be an employee or contractor
of the licensee; and shall be required to confirm the identification of the
purchaser.
vi. The licensee and!or the delivery service employee or contractor shall notify the
Cheyenne Police Department of any criminal incident or criminal activity
which is observed or occurs as soon as reasonably possible.
vii. All sales shall occur on the licensee’s premise by in-person purchase, online
order, order by app, or order by phone. Payment shall be received at the time
of purchase. Payment may not occur upon delivery.
viii.
Licensees shall keep a record of each delivery service transaction for a
period of no less than two (2) years which shall be made available to the city
upon request. The record shall include, at minimum, the name and address of
the purchaser, date and time of order, address of delivery, name of delivery
employee or contractor, and the product purchased and delivered.
ix. Delivery shall only be made to the purchaser.
x.
Delivery shall be prohibited for kegs.
xi. Delivery shall be completed the day of the sale.
4.
All retail liquor licensees, microbrewery permittees, winery permittees, satellite
winery permittees, and satellite manufacturing permittees, may establish a delivery service without
submitting an amended operation plan, provided that the licensee submits written notice to the city
clerk of its intent to provide delivery services. Failure to provide notice shall prohibit the licensee
from providing delivery services.
5.
All retail liquor licensees, microbrewery permittees, winery permittees, satellite
winery permittees, and satellite manufacturing permittees, may establish curbside pick-up services
at the licensed premise without submitting an amended operation plan.
6.
Any violation of the aforementioned requirements, restrictions, or procedures by
any licensee, licensee’s employee, or contractor shall be a misdemeanor punishable pursuant to
the general penalty provisions set forth in Chapter 1.24.010 of the city code.
7.
Should any portion of this ordinance be in conflict with any other city of Cheyenne
ordinance, this ordinance is deemed to control or supersede.
5.12.160
Violations.
E. No person may engage in the business of a delivery service involving alcoholic liquor
and malt
Prn-O
(The current Section F. shall be renumbered to Section E.)
Section 2.
That sections currently existing in Chapter 5.12 not set out within this
ordinance remain unchanged.
Section 3.
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect on July 1, 2021.
2

ORDINANCE NO.____________ 
 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT CODE 
(UDC) TO ALIGN THE UDC FEE SCHEDULE WITH THE FEE 
ADOPTION PRACTICES OF THE CITY.”  
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
that is the subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2. That the intent of this text amendment is to align the UDC fee schedule with the 
fee adoption practices of the City. 
 
Section 3. That on May 3, 2021 the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 4. That Section 1.3.7 is amended as follows; “No action shall be taken on any 
application required by these regulations unless all fees have been paid in full. A schedule of 
fees, charges and expenses shall be established by ordinance of the City Council after a public 
hearing, and may be amended periodically. Fees shall be required at the time of the filing of 
any development application and are payable to the City in accordance with the fee 
schedules adopted by the City Council to defray some or all of the cost of processing land 
development applications of this Code. The City Council may amend the fee schedule from 
time-to-time.  The fee schedule shall be made available to the public on the City website.” 
 
Section 5. The aforementioned Section of the UDC is hereby amended as set out within 
this ordinance and sections currently existing within the UDC not set out within this ordinance 
remain unchanged. 
 
Section 6. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable.  If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional, such unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, 
paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 7. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and 
publication.

SECOND READING:__ __________________________________

PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR 
(SEAL)

KRISTINA F. JONES, CITY CLERK 
 
By Alessandra at 8:18 am, Apr 21, 2021

 
 
2 
 
Published:_____________________ 
Wyoming Tribune Eagle 

PLN-21-00003  
 
 
May 3, 2021 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   (Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
PLANNING COMMISSION  
MAY 3, 2021 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code of the City of 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, to align the UDC fee schedule with the fee adoption 
practices of the City. 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLN-21-00003 
PREPARED BY: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
to align the Unified Development Code fee schedule with the fee adoption practices of the City, as outlined 
in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the request meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012. The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of Plan Cheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
UDC 1.3.7 establishes a process for the establishment of fees associated with applications associated with 
the Planning and Development office.  The present text of the Ordinance (UDC 1.3.7) reads as follows:  
“No action shall be taken on any application required by these regulations unless all fees have 
been paid in full. A schedule of fees, charges and expenses shall be established by ordinance of 
the City Council after a public hearing, and may be amended periodically.” 
 
DISCUSSION: 
 
City staff has been working together to establish a universal fee schedule and amending this section of 
the UDC will allow the Governing Body to establish a fee schedule as a resolution. The UDC requirement 
that the fee adoption be done by ordinance can be viewed as overly burdensome due to the amount of 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLN-21-00003  
 
 
May 3, 2021 
 
time to approve an ordinance and the fiscal costs to publish the ordinance upon approval.  Additionally, 
if adopted by ordinance, an amendment to the fee schedule would also require an amendment by 
ordinance. Fees were last adopted by Resolution 3451 in 1993. 
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENT: 
 
The proposed amendment is rather straightforward. The amendment would modify UDC Section 1.3.7 
as follows; “No action shall be taken on any application required by these regulations unless all fees 
have been paid in full. A schedule of fees, charges and expenses shall be established by ordinance of the 
City Council after a public hearing, and may be amended periodically. Fees shall be required at the time 
of the filing of any development application and are payable to the City in accordance with the fee 
schedules adopted by the City Council to defray some or all of the cost of processing land 
development applications of this Code. The City Council may amend the fee schedule from time-to-
time.  The fee schedule shall be made available to the public on the City website.”  
 
(Staff notes the term “City Council” includes the “Governing Body” pursuant to UDC 1.2.4.) 
 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or is 
necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 
This proposed text amendment is consistent with the Foundation 7, and principles and policies within 
PlanCheyenne. Specifically, the proposed amendment addresses the following policies and principles: 
 
Foundation 7 – Developing in a Fiscally Responsible Way 
Synopsis: The Cheyenne Area excels in providing services in a fiscally responsible way…  The 
Plan aims to guide future growth to promote efficient use of public and private resources and 
to provide adequate public facilities to sustain our quality of life and to encourage continued 
growth. This includes having reasonable fees to offset costs associated with development 
review.  
 
Principle 7.2: Adequate Public Services 
Synopsis: The Cheyenne Area will provide adequate public facilities and services for 
current and future residents in a fiscally responsible manner. 
 
Policy 7.2.C: Maintain Level of Service  
Synopsis: Coordinate with service providers and other governmental organizations that 
provide services to community residents to ensure that existing and new neighborhoods have 
adequate services, and that existing public facilities are properly maintained to serve the 
needs of current and future residents.  
 
Principle 7.5:  Funding of Quality of Life Services 
Synopsis: Our community will consider how to fund quality of life services in an 
effective and efficient manner. 

PLN-21-00003  
 
 
May 3, 2021 
 
 
Policy 7.5.A: Funding for Quality of Life Services 
Synopsis: Continue to identify and provide funding for services and amenities that contribute 
to quality of life and economic vitality of the community, including parks and recreation, 
cultural and library facilities, and other services. 
 
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-602 (a):  
The governing body shall specify how regulations, restrictions and the 
district boundaries are to be determined, established, enforced, 
amended, supplemented or otherwise changed. 
Assessment:  
State statute states that the governing body shall specify how regulations 
regarding zoning are established and enforced.  The Governing Body 
possesses the authority to amend regulations and therefore can amend 
the UDC to reflect this text amendment. 
 
WYO 15-1-601 (a):  
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
(i) Regulate and restrict the: 
(D) Density of the population 
(E) Location and use of buildings, structures and land for trade, 
industry, residence or other purposes. 
Assessment:  
This amendment proposes modifying the UDC for the purposes of 
updating development fees associated with development applications 
regarding the location of buildings and density of the population; thus, 
fulfilling the requirements of this statute.  
 
WYO 15-1-601(d):  
All regulations shall be made: 
(ii) With reasonable consideration, among other things, of the character 
of the district and its peculiar suitability for particular uses; 
(iii) With a view to conserving the value of buildings and encouraging the 
most appropriate use of land throughout the city or town. 
Assessment: 
This amendment proposes modifying the UDC for the purposes of 
encouraging simple public processes to establish and modify fees related 
to development applications. Regulations of this nature are allowable 
under state statue and may be modified by the Governing Body.    
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment will not adversely impact the general health, safety and public welfare of 
Cheyenne. 
 
 
 
 

PLN-21-00003  
 
 
May 3, 2021 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. Allowing modification 
of the fee schedule by resolution allows for a timely process open to the public and is consistent with past 
practices. The amendments accomplish several policies within PlanCheyenne and are enabled by 
Wyoming State Zoning statutes. Lastly, updating the process for establishment of fees will not jeopardize 
the overall health, safety, and welfare of the community. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to Unified Development Code Section 1.3.7 to align the 
UDC fee schedule with the fee adoption practices of the City as outlined in this staff report, noting the 
request meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 


--- END OF FILE ---

of the persons residing in the area and the City of Cheyenne; and 
(d) 
That the urban development of the area sought to be annexed will constitute a 
natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and other 
services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, be 
furnished; and
(f)
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned A-1
Agricultural and Rural Residential by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 
(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and its
governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the entire annexed 
area. 
Section 2.
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
Section 3.
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to the 
official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 

 
Section 4. 
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5. 
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

CITY OF CHEYENNE GOVERNING BODY

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-21-00115:  Annexation of Saddle Ridge, 16th Filing  
REQUEST: 
An annexation of 4.3 acres of land  
LOCATION: 
North of Pershing Boulevard, west of Christensen Road.  
APPLICANT: 
WJE, LLC 
AGENT: 
John Sayers, WJE, LLC 
OWNER: 
WJE, LLC 
PURPOSE: 
To allow urban-level residential development 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00115
May 10, 2021
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing of 4.3 acres of land for future single-unit residential development. The 
County Assessor presently shows the tract of land as part of one larger unplatted parcel. The land is currently 
undeveloped in the County. A zone change and plat are proposed concurrently with this annexation. All three 
applications are anticipated to come to final consideration before the Governing Body at the same meeting.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by a submission 
to change the zoning from County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential to the City zone of MR Medium-
density Residential.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned A-1 Agricultural in the County. The property is concurrently proposed to 
be annexed and rezoned to the MR Medium-density Residential. The MR zone is generally described as a 
primarily residential zone with compact neighborhood development patterns. The district generally 
allows a variety of residential uses with some smaller-scale civic or commercial uses.      
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the occupants of 
future development once it is built. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to 
City Fire and Police and sanitation services. The property will also be connected to City water 
and sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.

family Residential

County A-1, County A-2

UDC-21-00115
May 10, 2021
2)
The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The contiguity to the west makes the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. The 
development of this property will benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the 
social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. City water and sewer lines exist in the vicinity and could be 
extended with the development of the property. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation is about 28.5% contiguous, along the western edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 .   
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on April 8, 2021. The public 
hearing for this annexation is scheduled for May 10, 2021. Twenty (20) business days prior to 
the announced public hearing is April 12, 2021. The earliest confirmation of receipt was dated 
April 9, 2021. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   

UDC-21-00115
May 10, 2021
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne anticipates this area of the community to develop with an 
urban residential style of development. These areas are expected to develop with full access to urban 
services  such areas are best served by being within City limits. Policies in PlanCheyenne support 
development within the Urban Service Boundary and the utilization of existing infrastructure.  
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were made encouraging annexation of the property for the 
provision of City services.    
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
services typically available to urban areas are available to this property.   
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property.

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM 
COUNTY A-1 AGRICULTURAL AND RURAL RESIDENTIAL TO MR 
MEDIUM-DENSITY RESIDENTIAL FOR LAND LOCATED NORTH OF 
PERSHING BOULEVARD, WEST OF CHRISTENSEN ROAD.” 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING:

of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code (“UDC”).

approval of said zone change. 
Section 3. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
Section 4. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as:

long chord bears N20°55'27", a distance of 28.28 feet to a point; thence continuing along the 
easterly line of Saddle Ridge 13th Filing, N10°37'24”W, a distance of 61.69 feet to a point; thence

FILE:
UDC-21-00116: Saddle Ridge, 16th Filing Zone Change 
 
REQUEST:

Agricultural and Rural Residential to MR Medium-density Residential in 
concert with an annexation     
LOCATION: 
Northwest of the intersection of Pershing Boulevard and Christensen 
Road 
AGENT: 
John Sayers  WJE, LLC  
OWNER: 
WJE, LLC 
ZONING: 
County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Urban Residential 
PURPOSE: 
To give land a City zone in concert with annexation 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development  
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00116
May 3, 2021
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of a portion of the east half of Section 25, Township 14 North, Range 66 
West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming from County A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential to 
MR Medium-density Residential as shown in the attached zone change map, noting that the project 
meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County A-1 Agricultural and Rural 
Residential to MR Medium-density Residential for 4.6 acres of vacant land in concert with an annexation. The 
land is anticipated to developed as a single-unit residential uses, with associated streets and open areas.     
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary and existing 
infrastructure exists in the neighborhoods to the west. Development will require infrastructure 
extensions and improvements.  The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Urban 
Residential. The optimal zoning for the proposed future land use designation is any of the 
residential zones, including the MR zone. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
Future Land Use Designation  
Zoning 
Land Use 
Subject Site 
Urban Residential 
County A-1 
Undeveloped 
North 
Urban Residential 
County A-1 
Undeveloped 
South 
Urban Residential 
County AR 
Low density Single-
family Residential
East 
Urban Residential 
County A-1, County A-2 
Rural Residential 
West 
Urban Residential 
MR, P   
Single-family Residential 

UDC-21-00116
May 3, 2021
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
The existing zoning to the south is AR and outside of City limits. The property to the north and 
east is zoned A-1 Agricultural and Rural Residential, and is also outside of City limits. The 
properties to the west are zoned MR Medium-density Residential and P Public District within 
City limits. The future land uses anticipated by the Comprehensive Plan and other official plans 
for this area anticipate higher-intensity, urban-style residential uses, and the MR zone is both 
compatible with existing zoning in the area and with the future land use designation.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current County A-1 zone allows limited development options and is intended for low-
intensity rural uses. Generally, parcels in this zone district are not expected to connect to urban 
services. The proposed MR zone allows a variety of more urban development options. Also, as 
the property is being annexed into the City, a City zone is required. 
   
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 
 
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The standards of Article 4 apply to MR zoning. Due to the undeveloped nature of the land to the 
north and east, the subject property would be expected to meet all the standards of Article 4. 
The zone change is submitted in concert with a platting application, and the associated plat 
shows the area developing in accordance with the standards of Article 4.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The MR zone is intended to develop with a variety of urban-style residential uses. The MR zone 
is applicable to areas designated Urban Residential by the Future Land Use Map, and is intended 
to be within ½ mile of commercial centers. This property is anticipated to develop with urban-
style uses in the future. The property is within a mile of existing commercial centers located at 
Campstool and Christensen and at US 30 and Whitney. The property is about ½ mile away from 
an anticipated commercial center which would be located at the intersection of US 30 and 
Christensen.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    

UDC-21-00116
May 3, 2021
6)
The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The applicant is seeking the proposed zone change in concert with an annexation. The proposed 
MR zone is the zoning for nearby non-public properties within City limits. A specific 
development pattern is proposed with this application, but should that application not be 
executed, the MR zoning provides a variety of development options compatible with the 
surrounding neighborhoods. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
No reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
The application conforms to PlanCheyenne per the discussion with Review Criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no issues were identified by staff, and no comments regarding the 
proposed zone change were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The zoning map 
amendment application is proposed in concert with an annexation, and the MR zone is the City zone 
contiguous to the subject property for non-public development, and other nearby City development 
utilizes the MR zone. The proposed MR zone is also consistent with the future development patterns 
associated with the future land use designation. Staff recommends approval of the zoning map 
amendment as submitted by the applicant. 
 
 
 

UDC-21-00116
May 3, 2021
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant (Staff 
Recommendation). 
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications. 
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County A-1 to MR.

2. Master Application 
3. Applicant Provided Zone Change Map

(d)

natural, geographical, economical and social part of the City of Cheyenne; and 
(e) 
That the area is a logical and feasible addition to the City and that the extension of 
basic services such as water and sewer systems, police and fire protection and other 
services customarily available in the City of Cheyenne can, within reason, be 
furnished; and
(f)
That the area is adjacent to the City of Cheyenne and is currently zoned A-2
Agricultural by the Laramie County Commissioners; and 

(g) 
That the City of Cheyenne does not own or operate its own electrical utility, and its 
governing body is prepared to designate a utility to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410, and to authorize the designated utility to serve the entire annexed 
area. 
 
Section 2. 
That all of the real property described in Exhibit A is hereby annexed to the 
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, and shall become a part thereof.  
Section 3. 
That the City Clerk is directed to file an executed copy of this annexation 
ordinance and map (Exhibit A) with the County Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for 
Laramie County, Wyoming, and shall also notify the City Engineer's Office to add said area to the 
official City Map of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming. 
 
Section 4. 
Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any phrase, clause, sentence, paragraph or section of this ordinance is declared illegal 
or unconstitutional, such illegality or unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining 
phrases, clauses, sentences, paragraphs and sections.  
 
Section 5. 
That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its publication.

APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.4.2 Annexations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting annexing of 481 acres of land for future industrial development. The County 
Assessor presently shows the land as part of three unplatted parcels. The land is currently undeveloped in 
the County. A zone change and plat are proposed concurrently with this annexation. All three applications 
are anticipated to come to final consideration before the Governing Body at the same meeting.  
 
Land annexed to the City requires a City zone. The petition to annex has been accompanied by a submission 
to change the zoning from County A-2 Agricultural and Rural Residential to the City zones of AG Agricultural, 
LI Light Industrial, and HI heavy Industrial.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned A-2 Agricultural in the County. The property is concurrently proposed to 
be annexed and rezoned to the AG Agricultural, LI Light Industrial, and HI heavy Industrial zones. The AG 
zone is generally described as a primarily agricultural zone intended for use where land is to be held in a 
natural state or as a holding zone until development is appropriate.  The HI and LI zones are intended for 
industrial development and is appropriate near regional transportation routes and to larger land areas 
that are capable of providing substantial buffers and located remote from other differing and 
incompatible land uses. 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
To accept the petition to annex and approve the annexation Ordinance, the Governing Body must make 
the following findings required in WS 15-1-402; a detailed analysis of which is below: 
 
1) An annexation of the area is for the protection of the health, safety and welfare of the 
persons residing in the area and in the city or town.  [WS 15-1-402 (a) (i)] 
 
The annexation of this property will increase the health, safety, and welfare of the occupants of 
future development once it is built. By being in City limits, the property will receive access to

UDC-21-00125
May 10, 2021
City Fire and Police and sanitation services. The property will also be connected to City water 
and sanitary sewer instead of having wells and septic systems.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
2) The urban development of the area sought to be annexed would constitute a natural, 
geographical, economical and social part of the annexing city or town. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (ii)] 
 
This property, once developed, will constitute a natural, geographic, economic, and social part 
of the city. The contiguity to the north makes the parcel a natural geographic part of the City. 
The property is also adjacent to a major transportation route servicing the City. The 
development of this property will benefit those who live within the City and will be a part of the 
social and economic fabric that is Cheyenne.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
3) The area sought to be annexed is a logical and feasible addition to the annexing city or town 
and the extension of basic and other services customarily available to residents of the city or 
town shall, within reason, be available to the area proposed to be annexed. [WS 15-1-402 (a) 
(iii)] 
 
The area is a logical and feasible addition to the City due to the reasons previously listed. All City 
services normally available to residents will also be made available. Police and fire protection 
will be extended upon annexation. City water and sewer lines are planned to be extended to the 
area and with the development of the property. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
4) The area sought to be annexed is contiguous with or adjacent to the annexing city or town, or 
the area meets the requirements of W.S. 15-1-407. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (iv)] 
 
The area proposed for annexation are about 33.7% contiguous, along the northern edge.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 
5) If the city or town does not own or operate its own electric utility, its governing body is 
prepared to issue one (1) or more franchises as necessary to serve the annexed area pursuant 
to W.S. 15-1-410. [WS 15-1-402 (a) (v)] 
 
Utility franchises are in place to serve the annexed area.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
 .   
6) The annexing city or town, not less than twenty (20) business days prior to the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a), has sent by certified mail to all landowners and affected public 
utilities within the territory a summary of the proposed annexation report as required under 
subsection (c) of this section and notice of the time, date and location of the public hearing 
required by W.S. 15-1-405(a). [WS 15-1-402 (a) (vi)] 
 
A certified mailing was made to the landowners and public utilities on April 8, 2021. The public 
hearing for this annexation is scheduled for May 10, 2021. Twenty (20) business days prior to 

UDC-21-00125
May 10, 2021
the announced public hearing is April 12, 2021. The earliest confirmation of receipt was dated
April 9, 2021. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
   
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
Plan Cheyenne 
 
The Future Land Use Map of PlanCheyenne anticipates this area of the community to develop with an 
urban residential style of development. These areas are expected to develop with full access to urban 
services  such areas are best served by being within City limits. Policies in PlanCheyenne support 
development within the Urban Service Boundary and the utilization of existing infrastructure.  
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were made noting that clarification of jurisdiction on US 85 is 
needed.    
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the annexation.   
 
The proposed annexation is a natural extension of the City, is contiguous to the current City limits, and 
services typically available to urban areas are available to this property.   
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the annexation of the property.

ORDINANCE NO. ____________________ 
ENTITLED: “AN ORDINANCE AMENDING THE OFFICIAL ZONING MAP OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM 
COUNTY A-2 AGRICULTURAL TO AG AGRICULTURAL, LI LIGHT 
INDUSTRIAL, AND HI HEAVY INDUSTRIAL FOR LAND LOCATED EAST OF 
SOUTH GREELEY HIGHWAY (US 85), NORTH OF CHALK BLUFF ROAD.” 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
Section 1. That the owner(s) of the property described herein (with the exception of the property 
owned by Wyoming Department of Transportation) have applied for a zoning map amendment (“zone 
change”) for said property in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne 
Unified Development Code (“UDC”). 
Section 2. That City staff has petitioned City Council for a zoning map amendment (“zone 
change”) for the property owned by the Wyoming Department of Transportation on said property in 
accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, of the Cheyenne Unified Development Code 
(“UDC”).
Section 3. That on May 3, 2021, the City of Cheyenne Planning Commission conducted a public 
hearing, reviewed the proposed zone change that is the subject of this ordinance, and recommended 
approval of said zone change. 
Section 4. That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the zone change that is the 
subject of this ordinance meets the criteria specified in Section 2.2.1.d of the UDC. 
Section 5. That, in accordance with Section 2.2.1, Zoning Map Amendment, Section 5.1.2, Zoning 
Districts Established, and Section 5.1.3, Official Zoning Map, of the UDC, the aforementioned application 
is hereby approved and the zoning classification of the real property described as Lot 1, Block 2, Lot 1, 
Block 3, and Lot 2, Block 4, Bison Business Park, 2nd Filing is hereby changed to AG Agricultural; ; the 
zoning classification of the real property described as Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Bison Business Park, 2nd
Filing is hereby changed to LI Light Industrial; and the zoning classification of the real property described 
as Lot 1, Block 4, Bison Business Park, 2nd Filing is hereby changed to HI Heavy Industrial. 
Section 6. That the City Engineer is hereby authorized and directed to alter and amend the Official
Zoning Map as described in Section 5.1.3 of the UDC to comply with the zone change as described in 
Section 4 above when this ordinance takes effect in accordance with Section 8 below. 
Section 7. Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses and phrases of this ordinance are severable. If 
any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared unconstitutional, such 
unconstitutionality shall not affect any of the remaining sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and 
phrases.
Section 8. That this ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon its approval and publication, 
and upon the official filing of the affiliated Bison Business Park, 2nd Filing final plat map with the County 
Clerk and Ex-Officio Register of Deeds for Laramie County, Wyoming.

FILE:

REQUEST: 
A request to rezone approximately 351.2 acres of land from County A-2 
Agricultural to AG Agricultural, LI Light Industrial, HI heavy Industrial, and 
P Public District in concert with an annexation     
LOCATION: 
East of South Greeley Highway (US 85), north of Chalk Bluffs Road 
AGENT: 
Brad Emmons, AVI, PC  
OWNER: 
Arp and Hammond Hardware Company, Lummis Livestock Company, 
LLC, and Wyoming Department of Transportation 
ZONING: 
County A-2 Agricultural 
FUTURE LAND USE: 
Industrial 
PURPOSE: 
To give land City zoning in concert with annexation. 
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Planner II - Development  
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00126
May 3, 2021
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the rezoning of Lots 1 and 2, Block 1, Bison Business Park, Second Filing to LI (Light 
Industrial); Lot 1, Block 4, Bison Business Park, Second Filing to HI (Heavy Industrial); and Lot 1, Block 2, 
Lot 1 Block 3 and Lot 2, Block 4, Bison Business Park, Second Filing to AG (Agricultural) as shown in the 
attached zone change map, noting that the project meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.2.1 Zoning Map Amendment 
 
UDC Article 5 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
This zone change application proposes changing the zoning from County A-2 Agricultural to AG Agricultural, 
LI Light Industrial, HI heavy Industrial, and P Public District for 351.2 acres of mostly vacant land in concert 
with an annexation. The land is anticipated to be developed in the future with industrial-style uses and 
associated streets.    
 
  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Zoning Map Amendment, the 
Commission must make the following findings required in UDC 2.2.1.d. A detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan.  (UDC 2.2.1.d.1) 
 
PlanCheyenne encourages growth within the Urban Service Boundary and the maximization of 
existing infrastructure. This property is within the Urban Service Boundary, as recently modified 
by a recent update to the Future Land Use Map. Infrastructure does not currently exist in the 
area and development will require significant infrastructure extensions and improvements.

changed)

UDC-21-00126
May 3, 2021
The Future Land Use Designation for this property is Industrial (also updated with the recent 
change to the Future Land Use Map). The optimal zoning for the proposed future land use 
designation is LI Light Industrial or HI Heavy Industrial. 
 
While the proposed AG zone is not consistent with the land uses identified by the Future Land 
Use Map, the AG zone is the rough equivalent of the current County zone and would not have 
an adverse effect on the potential for the property to develop in accordance with the plans and 
policies of the comprehensive plan in the future.  
 
The P Public District is intended for public entities providing government services. The property 
proposed to be zoned P Public District is currently used by the Wyoming Department of 
Transportation and would be required to be rezoned in the event of a land transfer. Staff 
recommends this be zoned LI (Limited Industrial) Zone district for consistency with nearby 
zoning and to reflect the highly truck-oriented services at the site.  Zoning this LI will not require 
park fees (since it is a government use) and will not subject structures on the land to design 
standards.    
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 1. 
 
2) The application is compatible with nearby property, specifically considering existing zoning, 
the Comprehensive Plan, and any other official plans and policies created under the guidance 
of that plan for these areas; (UDC 2.2.1.d.2) 
 
To the south, this property borders land outside the zoned area. To the east and west, the 
existing zoning is mostly AR and outside of City limits. The property to the north is zoned AG and 
HI within City limits (a recently-approved change). The current zoning of the subject property is 
AR, and the AG zone is the City zone most analogous to the current county zone.  
 
The future land uses anticipated by the Comprehensive Plan for this area anticipate higher-
intensity, industrial uses. The LI and HI zones are the expected zoning designations for this type 
of development and are compatible with the long-range plan for the area. While the proposed 
AG zone is not consistent with the Future Land Use Map, the AG zone is intended to be able to 
proposed AG zone is 
consistent with existing County zoning in the area.   
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 2. 
   
3) The suitability of the subject property for development according to the standards and uses of 
the existing zoning district, compared to the suitability of the subject property for 
development according to the standards and uses of the proposed zoning district (UDC 
2.2.1.d.3) 
  
The current County AR zone allows limited development options and is intended for low-
intensity uses. Generally, parcels in this zone district are not expected to connect to urban 
services. The proposed AG zone is the City zone most analogous to the current County zone and 
also only allows limited development options. The proposed LI and HI zones allow higher-
intensity industrial uses, but are expected to develop with access to City infrastructure. 
Development of the property will require significant extension of infrastructure. 
   
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 3. 

UDC-21-00126
May 3, 2021
4) The ability of the subject property and surrounding vicinity to be developed according to the 
Subdivision Standards in Article 4 considering the proposed zoning (UDC 2.2.1.d.4) 
 
The standards of Article 4 have limited applicability to areas designated Industrial by the Future 
Land Use Map, and also limited in applicability to areas zoned AG. Minimum right-of-way widths 
and minimum street sections would still apply and would be reviewed with specific 
development actions.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 4. 
 
5) The ability of the application to support the Intent and Applicability statements of the 
proposed zoning district and achieve the Design Objectives of any specific standards. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.5) 
 
The HI zone is intended for uses of very high intensity which should be isolated and buffered in 
order to protect the community. Because the uses and site design can produce impacts beyond 
the property lines, the district should be located in more remote areas. It is appropriate near 
major regional transportation infrastructure and areas designated Industrial by the Future Land 
Use Map. 
 
The LI zone is intended for controlled manufacturing or intensive employment uses 
that may have accessory commercial, service or office uses, but which cannot readily fit into 
other general business or mixed-use areas due to the nature or intensity of operations. The LI 
district is applicable to larger areas that are capable of providing substantial buffers. 
 
The AG zone is intended to develop with primarily agricultural uses or to leave land in a natural 
state. This zone prohibits most of the typical urban-style uses.  The AG zone is applicable to 
areas where significant natural lands or open spaces are to be protected, or areas designated 
Agriculture/Rural or Rural Residential by the Future Land Use Map. The AG zone is also 
applicable as an interim or holding zone for areas that may be further developed in the long 
term, but where minimal development activity in the interim is acceptable and will not 
prematurely establish development patterns. 
 
This property is anticipated to develop with industrial uses in the future and the proposed HI 
and LI zones are appropriate and applicable in this location. The AG zone is appliable as a 
holding zone and is intended to leave the land in an undeveloped state  which would allow 
future rezoning and development of the property when development is appropriate. None of 
these three proposed districts have zone-specific design regulations. 
 
The P Public District is intended for governmental facilities serving the public. The parcel 
proposed to be zoned P is owned by the Wyoming Department of Transportation and is used as 
a Port-of-entry. The current ownership and use of the property makes P zoning acceptable, but 
as noted earlier in this staff report, staff believes LI is most appropriate for the WYDOT lot. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 5. 
    
6) The ability of all potential future development to meet all applicable standards for 
development in the proposed zoning district, including other development options if any 

UDC-21-00126
May 3, 2021
specific development proposed in association with the application is not executed after 
approval. (UDC 2.2.1.d.6) 
 
The applicant is seeking the proposed zone change in concert with an annexation. The proposed 
HI and LI zoning does not have zone-specific design standards beyond setback requirements, 
and the parcels proposed to be zoned LI and HI are large enough to allow development to meet 
the setback requirements. The proposed AG zoning is the zone most similar to the existing 
zoning on the property and on surrounding County properties. Development on the property 
zoned AG would require a future rezoning. 
 
While a specific development is proposed on the land to be zoned HI, the remainder does not 
currently have a development proposal. Should the specific development proposed for the HI 
parcel not be executed after the zone change is complete, the site is large enough to allow a 
variety of potential industrial uses. 
 
The proposed zone change complies with review criterion 6. 
 
7) The review and recommendations of other reviewing bodies. (UDC 2.2.1.d.7) 
 
the existing WYDOT facility may be served just as well by being zoned 
LI or HI, such a facility is not required to be zoned P. The LI or HI zone would make zoning more 
consistent, require less design and landscaping considerations for future development, and 
allow the redevelopment or re-use of the property without a zone change.  As noted earlier in 
this staff report, staff believes LI is most appropriate for the WYDOT lot (Lot 1, Block 1). 
 
No other reviewing agencies provided recommendations regarding the proposed zone change.  
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 7. 
 
 
8) Whether the application will harm the public health, safety, or general welfare. (UDC 
2.2.1.d.8) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed zone change would not adversely affect the public 
health, safety, or general welfare. 
 
The proposed zone change for the subject property complies with review criterion 8. 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
The application conforms to PlanCheyenne per the discussion with Review Criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, no issues were identified by staff, and no comments regarding the 
proposed zone change were received. 
 
CONCLUSION: 

UDC-21-00126
May 3, 2021
Staff is satisfied that the application meets the review criteria set forth in UDC 2.2.1.(d).  The zoning map 
amendment application is proposed in concert with an annexation. The LI and HI zones are appropriate 
based on the future land use designation for the subject property, and the AG zone is the zone most 
analogous to the current County zone for the subject property. Staff notes that as discussed in this staff 
LI.   As recommended by staff, the 
rezoning application still meets the review criteria for approval. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment as submitted by the applicant.  
2. Recommend approval of the zoning amendment subject to conditions or modifications. (Staff 
Recommendation). 
3. Recommend denial of the zoning amendment based on findings of denial. The Planning 
Commission must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the 
motion, findings for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the zoning amendment until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
 
If 
ecommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the 
Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the rezoning of the property from County AR to LI, HI, and AG subject to 
the following modification: 
1.
