RESOLUTION NO. _______________ 
 
 
ENTITLED:  “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE FILING OF AN APPLICATION 
WITH THE FEDERAL TRANSIT ADMINISTRATION FOR FEDERAL 
TRANSPORTATION ASSISTANCE AUTHORIZED BY TITLE 49, UNITED 
STATES CODE, SECTION 5339.” 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Federal Transportation Administrator has been delegated authority to 
award Federal financial assistance for transportation projects pursuant to 49 U.S.C. §5339; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the grant or cooperative agreement for Federal financial assistance will 
impose certain obligations upon the Applicant, and may require the Applicant to provide the 
local share of the project cost; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Applicant has or will provide all annual certifications and assurances 
required for the project to the Federal Transit Administration; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Applicant will apply to the Federal Transit Administration (FTA) for 
$1,040,000.00 of FTA Section 5339 funds for the purchase and renovation of a transit operations 
facility. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING: 
 
 
 
1.  That the Mayor is authorized to execute and file an application for Federal financial 
assistance in the amount of $1,040,000.00 on behalf of the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne 
Transit Program, with the Federal Transit Administration, as authorized by 49 U.S.C. §5339. 
 
 
2.  That the Mayor is authorized to execute and file with the application the annual 
certifications and assurances and other documents the Federal Transit Administration requires 
before awarding a Federal assistance grant or cooperative agreement. 
 
 
3.  That the mayor is authorized to execute grant and cooperative agreements with the 
Federal Transit Administration on behalf of the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Transit 
Program. 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Patrick Collins, Mayor, is designated as the 
authorized representative of the City of Cheyenne to act on behalf of the Governing Body on all 
matters relating to this grant application. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS _______ DAY OF ______________ 
2021. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
___________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick Collins, Mayor

___________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2021-06-01_TotalExtraction_66368522.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Mark Christensen, Planner II 
Subject: A Resolution Pursuant to Wyoming Statute § 15-9-106 Finding and Declaring Parts of the 
City of Cheyenne as Blight and Authorizing the Redevelopment Thereof. 
Date: 
June 9, 2021 
 
Background:  
The item for consideration is a Resolution.    
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
 
6/14/2021 
• 
Public Services Committee  
 
6/22/2021 
• 
Second and final reading  
 
6/28/2021 
 
Summary:  
 
The State of Wyoming authorizes communities to create Urban Renewal Authorities and summarizes the 
power of the authority in Wyoming Stat. § 15-9-101 through § 15-9-137. The powers authorized in the 
Wyoming Urban Renewal Code allow municipalities to declare areas as blighted and use tools, such as 
tax increment financing, to reinvest in these blighted areas. However, a municipality does not have the 
ability to exercise the authority in the Wyoming Urban Renewal Code until the local governing body 
adopts a resolution finding that one or more blighted areas exist in the municipality and the 
rehabilitation, conservation, redevelopment, or a combination is necessary for the public health, safety, 
morals or welfare of the residents of the municipality (WY Stat. § 15-9-106). This resolution serves to 
fulfill the requirements of WY Stat. § 15-9-106. Furthermore, the Planning and Development 
Department has created the attached blight study to overview the conditions summarized in the 
resolution. The study outlines the requirements as defined in State Statutes, introduces a methodology 
for determining blight, overviews three locations in the community where blight has been identified, 
and draws conclusions that the rehabilitation of the blighted areas is within the best interests of the 
community. It is important to note that Urban Renewal Projects may occur in more areas than the three 
identified in this study and this initiative resolution does not define a specific Urban Renewal Project or 
Plan. This study can serve as a model for determining future locations for Urban Renewal Plans and 
Projects. 
 
Attachments: 
1. Resolution 
2. Exhibit A: Conditions of Blight 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

RESOLUTION NO. ___________ 
 
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION PURSUANT TO WYOMING STATUTE § 15-9-106 
FINDING AND DECLARING PARTS OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AS 
BLIGHT AND AUTHORIZING THE REDEVELOPMENT THEREOF.” 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne desires to execute its authority under Wyoming 
Statute § 15-9-101, et seq., and begin the process of urban renewal within its boundaries; and  
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has conducted a study which finds certain areas within 
the City to be “blight” as defined by Wyoming Statute § 15-9-103(iii), with particularity many 
structures that constitute an economic or social liability or are a menace to the public health, 
safety, morals, or welfare in its present condition and use; and 
 
 
 
WHEREAS, the results of this study can be found in Exhibit A, attached hereto and 
incorporated herein by reference, and find the statutory conditions to be met; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute § 15-9-106 states the City of Cheyenne Governing Body 
must pass a resolution finding certain areas of the city to be blight; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne Governing Body does also declare that the 
rehabilitation, conservation, redevelopment, or a combination thereof of the areas defined in the 
attached Exhibit A are necessary in the interest of the public health, safety, morals, or welfare of 
the residents of the municipality; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the pursuant to Wyoming Statute §15-9-107, the City of Cheyenne 
Governing Body finds the areas defined in the attached Exhibit A to be appropriate for an urban 
renewal project; and  
 
 
WHEREAS, that the City of Cheyenne Governing Body hereby finds and declares part of 
the City of Cheyenne as detailed in the attached Exhibit A to be blight and authorizes the 
rehabilitation and redevelopment of these defined areas as necessary for the public safety, health, 
morals, and welfare for the residents of the City of Cheyenne.  
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne, 
Wyoming, hereby finds and declares part of the City of Cheyenne as detailed in the attached 
Exhibit A to be blight and authorizes the rehabilitation and redevelopment of these defined areas 
as necessary for the public safety, health, morals, and welfare for the residents of the City of 
Cheyenne. 
 
 
PRESENTED, READ, AND ADOPTED this ____ day of ________________, 2021. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
(SEAL) 
 
Attest: 
 
____________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

Conditions of Blight - Cheyenne | Page 1
Introduction
The State of Wyoming authorizes communities 
to create Urban Renewal Authorities and 
summarizes the power of the authority in 
Wyoming Stat. § 15-9-101 through § 15-9-137. 
The powers authorized in the Wyoming Urban 
Renewal Code allow municipalities to declare 
areas as blighted and use tools, such as tax 
increment financing, to reinvest in these blighted 
areas. However, a municipality does not have the 
ability to exercise the authority in the Wyoming 
Urban Renewal Code until the local governing 
body adopts a resolution finding that one or 
more blighted areas exist in the municipality and 
the rehabilitation, conservation, redevelopment, 
or a combination is necessary for the public 
health, safety, morals or welfare of the residents 
of the municipality (WY Stat. § 15-9-106). The 
Planning and Development Department has 
created this study to fulfill the requirements of 
WY Stat. § 15-9-106 and serve as an attachment 
for the initiative resolution. The study will outline 
requirements as defined in State Statutes, 
introduce a methodology for determining blight, 
overview three locations in the community where 
blight has been identified, and draw conclusions 
that the rehabilitation of the blighted areas is 
within the best interests of the community. It is 
important to note that Urban Renewal Projects 
may occur in more areas than the three identified 
in this study and this initiative resolution does not 
define a specific Urban Renewal Project or Plan. 
This study can serve as a model for determining 
future locations for Urban Renewal Plans and 
Projects.

Conditions of Blight - Cheyenne | Page 2
Wyoming State Statutes
The State of Wyoming authorizes communities 
to create Urban Renewal Authorities and utilize 
the power of the authority in Wyoming Stat. § 15-
9-101 through § 15-9-137. However, the sections 
relevant to the initiative resolution include WY 
Stat. § 15-9-103 and WY Stat. § 15-9-106. WY 
Stat. § 15-9-106, which state that the governing 
body must adopt an initiative resolution in order 
to exercise the power outlined in the Wyoming 
Urban Renewal Code. The section explains:
(a) No municipality shall exercise the authority 
conferred upon municipalities by this chapter 
until the local governing body, on its own motion 
or by virtue of a petition signed by twenty-five 
(25) or more electors of the municipality, has 
adopted a resolution finding that:
(i) One (1) or more slum or blighted areas exist 
in the municipality; and
(ii) 
The 
rehabilitation, 
conservation, 
redevelopment or a combination thereof of the 
area or areas is necessary in the interest of the 
public health, safety, morals or welfare of the 
residents of the municipality.
- WY Stat. § 15-9-106
To aide in determining what constitutes a slum 
or blighted area, WY Stat. § 15-9-103 contains 
the definition of “blighted area” and “slum area”. 
Specifically, the section describes a blighted 
area as:
(iii) “Blighted area” means an area which by 
reason of the presence of a substantial number 
of slums, deteriorated or deteriorating structures, 
predominance of defective or inadequate 
street layout, faulty lot layout in relation to size, 
adequacy, accessibility or usefulness, unsanitary 
or unsafe conditions, deterioration of site or 
other improvements, diversity of ownership, tax 
or special assessments, delinquency exceeding 
the fair value of the land, defective or unusual 
conditions of title, or the existence of conditions 
which endanger life or property by fire and other 
causes, or any combination of those factors, 
substantially impairs or arrests the sound 
growth of a municipality, retards the provision 
of housing accommodations or constitutes an 
economic or social liability and is a menace 
to the public health, safety, morals or welfare 
in its present condition and use. However, if 
the blighted area consists of open land, the 
conditions contained in W.S. 15-9-110(b) apply 
and any disaster area referred to in W.S. 15-9-
112 constitutes a “blighted area”;
- WY Stat. § 15-9-103
And goes on to define a slum area as:
(xvi) “Slum area” means an area in which there is 
a predominance of buildings or improvements, 
whether residential or nonresidential, which 
by reason of dilapidation, deterioration, age 
or obsolescence, inadequate provision for 
ventilation, light, air, sanitation, or open spaces, 
high density of population and overcrowding, 
or the existence of conditions which endanger 
life or property by fire and other causes, or any 
combination of those factors is conducive to ill 
health and is detrimental to the public safety, 
morals or welfare;
- WY Stat. § 15-9-103
This study uses the definitions to support the 
findings that WY Stat. § 15-9-106 requires. The 
remainder of the Wyoming Urban Renewal 
Code details the powers permitted by the 
Urban Renewal Authority and do not maintain 
substantial relevance to the initiative resolution; 
however, staff recommends the Governing Body 
familiarize themselves with these statutes.

Conditions of Blight - Cheyenne | Page 3
Methodology
To identify blight areas and slum areas in the 
community, staff created a survey with the nine 
(9) conditions of blight identified in the definitions 
section of the Wyoming Urban Renewal Code. 
Staff did not utilize the conditions of slum 
areas as they were similar to those of blight. 
Staff extrapolated nine distinct characteristics 
of blight as defined in WY Stat. § 15-9-103(iii). 
The nine conditions with one option of “Other” 
include:
•	 Deteriorated or Deteriorating Structures
•	 Predominance of Defective or Inadequate 	
	 Street Layout
•	 Faulty Lot Layout in Relation to Size, 	
	
	 Accessibility or Usefulness
•	 Unsanitary or Unsafe Conditions
•	 Deterioration of Site or Other Improvements
•	 Diversity of Ownership, Tax or Other 	
	
	 Special Assessments
•	 Delinquency Exceeding the Fair Value of 	
	 the Land
•	 Defective or Unusual Conditions of Title
•	 The Existence of Conditions which 	
	
	 Endanger Life or Property by Fire and other   
...causes
•	 Other
The other option pertained to the section of the 
definition that stated, “or any combination of 
those factors, substantially impairs or arrests 
the sound growth of a municipality, retards 
the provision of housing accommodations or 
constitutes an economic or social liability and 
is a menace to the public health, safety, morals 
or welfare in its present condition and use,” (WY 
Stat. § 15-9-103(iii)). After listing the conditions, 
the survey had the option for staff to use a 
check box for each condition that existed and 
to provide a description of indicators that 
were used to conclude that the condition was 
present. The end of the survey allowed staff to 
provide notes for anything of importance. Unlike 
other neighboring states, notably Colorado, 
the Wyoming Urban Renewal Code does not 
establish a threshold for the number of blight 
conditions that must be found to qualify an area 
for Urban Renewal. Based on this, if any one of 
the nine conditions are identified on the site, it is 
sufficient to determine the area as blighted.
Staff completed the blight surveys for three 
areas within the community on May 24, 2021 to 
comply with the WY Stat. § 15-9-106 provision 
of there being, “one (1) or more slum or blighted 
areas exist(ing) in the municipality.” Again, this 
study does not preclude other blighted areas 
from being surveyed in the future, rather serves 
as the initiation that permits the City to exercise 
the powers in WY Stat. § 15-9-101 et. seq.. The 
three areas surveyed include the Hitching Post 
and Vicinity, the Reed Avenue Rail Corridor, and 
the Hynds and the Hole. The three sites were 
surveyed on May 24th by one staff member for 
consistency. All locations contained conditions 
of blight, as detailed in the next section. 
Blight Surveys
This section of the report contains an overview 
of the blight surveys as conducted on May 24, 
2021. The blight categories present on site will 
be reviewed for each property, along with specific 
examples of the conditions that were witnessed 
at the time of the survey. The surveys and photos 
from the site visits are contained in Appendices 
A and B, respectively. Again, these areas are not 
designated as Urban Renewal projects through 
this process, rather they are illustrative of blight 
in the community.

Conditions of Blight - Cheyenne | Page 4
W. Lincolnway
Westland Road
Missile Drive
Figure 1: Hitching Post Study Area
Study Area Boundaries
1. Deteriorated or Deteriorating Structures
2. Predominance of Defective or 
Inadequate Street Layout
3. Faulty Lot Layout in Relation to Size, 
Accessibility or Usefulness
4. Unsanitary or Unsafe Conditions
5. Deterioration of Site or Other 
Improvements
6. The Existence of Conditions which 
Endanger Life or Property by Fire and other 
causes

Conditions of Blight - Cheyenne | Page 5
Hitching Post
The Hitching Post study area is shown in Figure 
1. The study area includes the Hitching Post 
property and several surrounding properties, 
including several across Lincolnway. The site 
contained six (6) of the nine (9) conditions of 
blight identified on the survey, demonstrating 
one area of blight in the community.
1. Deteriorated or Deteriorating Structures
The Hitching Post study area contains several 
examples of deteriorated or deteriorating 
structures, as shown in the photo on the previous 
page. Most of the buildings on the Hitching Post 
property are either boarded-up or burned, and 
the walkway between two of the buildings has 
partially collapsed. In addition to the buildings on 
the Hitching Post site, other buildings in the area 
show evidence of deterioration. The prevalence 
of deteriorating buildings as depicted in the 
supporting photographs in Appendix A fulfills 
this condition of blight. 
2. Predominance of Defective or Inadequate 
Street Layout
The 
Hitching 
Post 
study 
area 
contains 
approximately 50 acres. The subject area 
has very few connections and many of the 
connections present are access easements. The 
street network through the area is not sufficient 
for urban development and many connections 
will need to be planned and built.  Furthermore, 
the access easements are not built to city street 
standards and will need improvements in the 
future (shown in Figure 1, #2). Lastly, staff has 
had many discussions on the status of W. 18th 
Street and whether it has been vacated in the 
past, further complicating the street network in 
the area. Due to the faulty street network through 
the Hitching Post, the status of W. 18th Street, 
and the lack of connections through the Back 40 
subdivision, staff is satisfied this condition of 
blight is met.
3. Faulty Lot Layout in Relation to Size, 
Accessibility or Usefulness
Several of the parcels in the Hitching Post 
property are large with no lot frontage or direct 
right of way access. One of these is shown in 
Figure 1 with the Back 40 subdivision. Again, 
the lack of internal connections through the 
site lead to accessibility issues and a lack 
of pedestrian connections. The study area is 
designated as a Community/Regional Activity 
Center in PlanCheyenne, yielding a maximum 
block size with a 2000’ perimeter and 600’ 
maximum on any one block face per Unified 
Development Code Table 4-6. Currently, the site 
has approximately 1500’ of frontage on one 
block face and an incomplete block structure 
due to lack of connections; though, if one were 
to complete the block, it would be approximately 
5250’ of perimeter. The size of the blocks, 
lack of pedestrian routes, and lack of internal 
connections yield a faulty lot layout in relation to 
size, accessibility and usefulness; thus, meeting 
this condition of blight.
4. Unsanitary or Unsafe Conditions
A recent Phase I Environmental Site Assessment 
was completed at the Hitching Post in May of 
2021. The Phase I ESA reiterated that the site 
contains various unsafe conditions; including 
the presence of lead-based paint, asbestos-
containing 
building 
materials, 
mercury-
containing thermostats, and mold. In addition 
to the recognized environmental conditions 
on-site making the site unsafe and unsanitary, 
staff documented the presence of abandoned 
mattresses, trash, and fire damaged buildings 
(one instance shown in Figure 1, #4). There are 
also several un-connected sidewalks which lead 
to unsafe conditions for pedestrians. All the 
conditions noted meet the criteria for unsafe and 
unsanitary conditions as an indicator of blight at 
the property.

Conditions of Blight - Cheyenne | Page 6
5. Deterioration of Site or Other Improvements
Staff noted several conditions of deterioration 
of site improvements at the Hitching Post study 
area. The deterioration included curb, gutter, 
sidewalk, and areas around a storm sewer drain 
(Figure 1). In addition to these conditions of 
deterioration, staff also noted a downed light pole 
on the Hitching Post property. The deterioration 
of site conditions is illustrative of blight in the 
study area. 
6. The Existence of Conditions which Endanger 
Life or Property by Fire and Other Causes
The two main indicators of the site that meet 
these conditions include the fire-damaged 
building (Figure 1) and the presence of 
environmental contamination. A recent Phase 
I Environmental Site Assessment documented 
several conditions that are recognized as 
hazardous by the Environmental Protection 
Agency. The existence of fire-damaged building 
also leads to a concern, as the buildings have 
become unstable and possibly life endangering.

Conditions of Blight - Cheyenne | Page 8
Reed Avenue Rail Corridor
The Reed Avenue Rail Corridor is shown in Figure 
2. The study area includes the blocks between 
Bent Avenue and Snyder Avenue from east to 
west and between W 23rd Street and the Union 
Pacific Railroad from north to south. The study 
area included six (6) of the nine (9) conditions 
of blight identified in the survey, demonstrating a 
second area of blight in the community.  
1. Deteriorated or Deteriorating Structures
Staff noted several deteriorating buildings in the 
Reed Avenue Rail Corridor. Examples include 800 
W 17th Street (Figure 2) and 820 W 22nd Street; 
though several other buildings appear to be 
deteriorating and may be more so on the interior. 
Indicators of deterioration witnessed include 
evidence of fire, boarded-up buildings, and faulty 
building siding. The photo on the previous page 
and those in Appendix B illustrate the study area 
meets this condition of blight.
2. Faulty Lot Layout in Relation to Size, 
Accessibility or Usefulness
Many blocks in the study area contain platted 
alleys while others have been vacated. This 
leads to confusion and lack of usable area for 
development purposes. Supplemental to this 
evidence of blight, many lots have not been 
platted since the Original City subdivision. 
Staff has difficulty interpreting the Original City 
subdivision and the legal descriptions are often 
a source of confusion. The inconsistency in alley 
vacation and age of the subdivision lead to faulty 
lot layout in relation to size, accessibility, and 
usefulness. The photo in Figure 2 for this criteria 
depicts a lot with a faulty layout.
3. Unsanitary or Unsafe Conditions
The Reed Rail study area contained litter and a 
lack of connections over the rail tracks, meeting 
unsanitary conditions and unsafe conditions 
respectively. Furthermore, the prevalence of 
industrial businesses (Figure 2), and the age 
of the buildings in the area allude to possible 
environmental contamination. Several buildings 
in the area, including the Steam Plant, have had 
Environmental Site Assessments completed 
that confirm environmental contamination. The 
litter, lack of connections, and environmental 
contamination 
confirm 
the 
presence 
of 
unsanitary and unsafe conditions throughout 
the Reed Avenue Rail Corridor. 
4. Deterioration of Site or Other Improvements
The Reed Avenue Rail Corridor has deteriorated  
curb, gutter, and sidewalk in various locations 
(Figure 2). The deteriorated site conditions 
have exacerbated the pedestrian connections 
along the Reed Avenue Rail Corridor, which is 
a critical project for the City of Cheyenne. The 
photos in Appendix B show several locations of 
deteriorated site conditions and infrastructure. 
5. Diversity of Ownership, Tax or Other Special 
Assessments
The Reed Rail Corridor study area contains a 
wide variety of ownership, which makes planning 
holistically difficult. A wide variety of ownership 
fosters many different perspectives and needs 
to be met. The corridor contains both public and 
private ownership, as well. It is also noteworthy 
that there are several owners that reside out of 
state. 
6. The Existence of Conditions which Endanger 
Life or Property by Fire and Other Causes
The building located at 820 W 22nd Street 
shows evidence of fire damage (Figure 2). 
Furthermore, many of the vehicles adjacent 
to this site show evidence of fire. Again, the 
confirmed environmental contamination on 
some sites and probable contamination along 
the corridor yield another condition that may 
endanger life and property. The noted fire 
damage and environmental contamination fulfill 
this condition of blight.

Conditions of Blight - Cheyenne | Page 9
Figure 3: The Hynds and the Hole Study Area
Study Area Boundaries
3. The Existence of Conditions which 
Endanger Life or Property by Fire and other 
causes
2. Unsanitary or Unsafe Conditions
1. Deteriorated or Deteriorating Structures

Conditions of Blight - Cheyenne | Page 10
The Hynds and the Hole
The Hynds and the Hole study area is shown in 
Figure 3. The study area contains Lot 1, Block 
1 of Cheyenne Leads Lincolnway Subdivision 
(the Hole) and Lot 8, Block 390 of the Original 
City Subdivision (the Hynds). The study area 
included three (3) of the nine (9) conditions of 
blight identified in the survey, demonstrating a 
third area of blight in the community. 
1. Deteriorated or Deteriorating Structures
The Hynds and Hole exhibit both a deteriorated 
and deteriorating structure (Figure 3). The 
building where the Hole is burned down in 2004 
and the foundation that is still present shows 
evidence of deterioration. The Hynds building 
shows evidence of deterioration with rust and 
other loose and collapsing building materials.  
2, Unsanitary or Unsafe Conditions
The presence of the Hole represents an unsafe 
condition. Although the Hole is fenced off, if 
individuals are to enter over the fence or through 
the alley there is the possibility of injury. 
3. The Existence of Conditions which Endanger 
Life or Property by Fire and Other Causes
The Hole was created by a fire that destroyed the 
building in 2004. The site has remained vacant 
ever since and represents a blighted area in the 
community (Figure 3).

Conditions of Blight - Cheyenne | Page 11
Conclusions
The blight study at three locations illustrates 
that one or more blighted areas exist in the 
municipality, therefore fulfilling WY Stat. § 
15-9-106(i). The rehabilitation, conservation, 
redevelopment, or a combination thereof of 
the area or areas is necessary in the interest 
of the public health, safety, morals, or welfare 
of the residents of the municipality for various 
reasons. The City of Cheyenne governing body 
has the ability to use the powers made available 
through the Wyoming Urban Renewal Code 
to remedy the conditions of blight, should the 
accompanying resolution of blight be adopted. 
The redevelopment of the study areas is 
necessary for the public health, safety, and 
welfare in the following ways:
Hitching Post Study Area: The Hitching Post study 
area is located along  W. Lincolnway, which serves 
as a primary gateway to the City of Cheyenne. 
The conditions of blight found at the Hitching 
Post are not only detrimental to the aesthetics of 
the community, but also are dangerous to those 
that frequent the site. Staff found evidence of 
transient populations frequenting the area. Any 
person that visits the site is put in harms way by 
the condemned buildings on site. Furthermore, 
the lack of connections, faulty lot layout, and 
deteriorating pedestrian infrastructure make the 
site unattractive for redevelopment. The ability 
of the City to use Urban Renewal at the Hitching 
Post is necessary to redevelop and rehabilitate 
the site and vicinity to correct these conditions of 
blight for the health, safety, morals, and welfare 
of the community.
Reed Avenue Rail Corridor: The Reed Avenue 
Rail Corridor study area encompasses a priority 
redevelopment area in the City of Cheyenne. 
Staff noted the existence of six conditions of 
blight throughout the Rail Corridor that inhibit 
the redevelopment of the area from coming to 
fruition. The diversity of ownership and faulty 
lot layout make redevelopment difficult and 
perpetuate the other conditions of blight that 
comprise the health, safety, welfare, and morals 
of the community. Furthermore, the taxpayers 
of the community voted to fund the public 
improvements within the corridor, showing 
their approval the redevelopment of the area. 
The use of the Wyoming Urban Renewal Code 
would compliment the taxpayer dedication to 
the redevelopment of the corridor and promote 
the health, safety, welfare, and morals of the 
community.
The Hynds and the Hole: The Hynds and the Hole 
study area is located directly across from the 
Cheyenne Depot Plaza, which serves as the most 
prominent gathering space in the community. 
The conditions of blight affect the health, safety, 
welfare, and morals of those who frequent this 
prominent community location. The danger 
and aesthetic harm caused by the blight can be 
remedied through the use of the Wyoming Urban 
Renewal Code by making rehabilitation attractive 
to the development community. 
All three locations exemplify the existence of 
blight in the community. The rehabilitation, 
conservation, 
or 
redevelopment 
of 
each 
location would not only benefit the community, 
but is necessary for the health, safety, welfare, 
and morals of the community. The use of the 
Wyoming Urban Renewal Code can facilitate the 
redevelopment of each of these areas through 
several of the tools enabled by the legislation, 
namely tax increment financing. Staff is satisfied 
the report meets and exceeds the mandate for 
the initiative resolution by the governing body 
found in WY Stat. § 15-9-106 and recommends 
approval of the resolution.

BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for a 17.75-acre tract of land to create 60 lots for 
future residential and commercial development. The County Assessor presently shows the tract of land as 
one ownership parcel.  The land is undeveloped.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned County LR Low-density residential and County MU Mixed Use. The 
proposed zoning is City LR Low-density Residential, City MR Medium-density Residential and City CB 
Community Business for the commercial parcel.

UDC-21-00095

ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the preliminary plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, the subject land has been designated as 
Urban Transition Residential and Mixed-use Commercial. The Mixed-use Commercial portion is 
at the intersection of Dell Range and Whitney Road. 
 
Surrounding areas to the north and east are also designated Urban Transition Residential. To the 
south, the future land use designation around the intersection of Whitney Road and Dell Range 
Boulevard is designated Mixed-use Commercial, and to the west, the future land use 
designations include Mixed-use Commercial and Urban Residential (recently updated through 
the Connect 2045 update). 
 
The Urban Transition Residential designation encourages a range of lower-intensity residential 
uses. The optimal City zoning is LR or RR for this designation, and most of the area of this plat in 
the Urban Transition Residential designation is proposed to be zoned LR. A portion is proposed 
to be zoned MR. The MR zoning provides a transition from the higher-intensity commercial 
designation to the lower-intensity Urban Transition Designation. The plat proposes meeting the 
LR and MR zoning standards, which would be in conformance with the future land use map.  
 
The future land use map includes a mixed-use designation at the intersection of Whitney and 
Dell Range, indicating that a commercially-zoned property at this location is acceptable.  
 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Foundation 7 of PlanCheyenne support the use of existing infrastructure and timely provision of 
services (including emergency and education services). Large developments on the edge of the 
community can create issues with providing services in an effective and timely fashion. 
Development of this area of the community creates the need to improve street and emergency 
service infrastructure. Staff recommends that preliminary plats expanding the response area 
include conditions for agreements with the City regarding impacts to Cheyenne Fire Rescue and 
the design and construction of roadway improvements. 
 
Other principles of PlanCheyenne encourage tying neighborhoods together and a compatible 
development pattern between neighborhoods. Due to topographic constraints and previous 
development, the ability for the proposed subdivision to tie into the land to the north and east 
is limited. While the subdivision to the northeast is a largely rural subdivision, the LR zoning of 
the properties immediately adjacent to this plat and the proposed LR zoning of the plat provide 
a transition from rural residential to a more urban development pattern. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.  
 

UDC-21-00095
June 7, 2021
2)
The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed plat is adjacent to existing developments to the north and east, and adjacent to 
major transportation corridors to the south and west. The plat proposes tying into an existing 
intersection to the south, and into a proposed intersection to the west. Due to topography and 
previous development patterns, there is no real opportunity to tie into the neighborhood to the 
northeast. The preliminary plat shows the applicant s intent to tie into and extend utilities.  
 
should be located across from Haunted instead of Woodhouse. Placing the intersection to align 
with Woodhouse may limit the turning movements at that intersection.  
 
The narrative submitted with the plat proposes a trail along Dell Range and Whitney, which 
would help meet open space requirements and connect neighborhoods. The plat may have the 
opportunity to preserve some open space in topographically challenging areas, but none is 
proposed with this platting action. The applicant has indicated that the nearby Whitney Ranch 
4th Filing may provide enough open space for both  projects; this may be possible, however, the 
applicants would need to demonstrate that the open space provided satisfies the requirement 
for both projects and an agreement should be in place demonstrating that Whitney Ranch, 4th 
 open space.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.  
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Transportation Network: 
This plat proposes connecting into Whitney and Dell Range at three points. Due to topographic 
constraints and previous development, connections to the north and east are not proposed. 
 
Maximum and minimum block dimensions are established by the Unified Development Code. 
Blocks within this proposed subdivision do not meet the block standards put forth in the code; 
however, these blocks appear to qualify for exceptions due to the constraints in place. Due to 
the significant topographic change, Staff recommends that Block 3 qualifies for the Natural 
Features exception.  
 
Single tier blocks are generally prohibited, but may be allowed with specific City Council 
lots with frontages on two parallel streets  one street in the front and one in the rear of the lot. 
Lots of this nature can create issues with access, addressing, setbacks, and maintenance. In this 
plat, there are lots along both Dell Range and Whitney which fit this description. The applicant 
has proposed that a Greenway be located along Dell Range. A Greenway is generally maintained 
by the City, so this may mitigate some of the potential maintenance issues. To mitigate some of 
the other issues presented by single-tier blocks, the applicant should provide a note to the plat 
prohibiting lots from being accessed off of Dell Range and Whitney. The City Council may also 
-of-way or easements to mitigate the 
a
 Staff recommends that a 

UDC-21-00095
June 7, 2021
common fencing plan be provided with final plat submittal. While Block 4 has double-fronted 
lots, due to the constraints, such lots may be appropriate.  
 
The UDC recommends that similar lots face each other across the public streetscape. For the 
most part, this plat fulfills this objective. A couple of residential lots east of Woodhouse would 
face the commercial parcel. Lots along Porter east of Woodhouse have MR-zoned duplex lots 
facing LR-zoned single-family lots across the public street. In the latter case, however, the 
development pattern is still single-unit residential development on individually owned parcels. 
 
The Porter Drive right-of-way proposed with this plat is at the minimum street section allowed 
by the UDC, however, the Engineering Department has determined that a wider right-of-way is 
needed to effectively develop the street within this portion of the community  
-
of-
 not meet standard street 
sections. Street right-of-way widths and street sections are established to allow for proper 
traffic flow, pedestrian connections, and on-street parking. Substandard widths may not be able 
to provide sufficient room for street improvements. 
 
Community Facility Fees: 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. The 
required fees for the 14.6 acres of non-exempt land in this plat are as follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$5,110 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
 
$5,110 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$10,220.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Commercial Enhancement Fee 
$1,100 per acre 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
 
Open Space: 
Open space is required to be provided with this subdivision. For low-density residential 
development, open space is required at 750 square feet per dwelling unit or 8% of the gross plat 
area (whichever is less).  
 
Commercial and mixed-use open space is required based on the size of the building. Because 
building footprints and total number of multifamily units are not determined until site plans are 
submitted, multifamily, mixed-use, and commercial development are generally expected to 
provide open space at time of site plan. Approximately 3.6 acres of the plat area is proposed as 
multifamily, mixed-use, or commercial development. 
 
This plat proposes 59 single-unit parcels with a gross acreage of roughly 14 acres (excepting the 
multifamily, mixed-use, and commercial lot acreage). The required open space is about 1.1 
acres. The applicant
 proposes providing 
trail corridor along Whitney and 
Dell Range. Extra land dedicated to right-of-way for the purposes of a trail corridor is one way to 
meet the open space requirement, but the proposed plat is deficient in meeting the open space 
requirement. 
Whitney Ranch, 4th Filing plat  this open space is yet to be dedicated or developed, but if taken 
on the whole, the open space provided with Whitney Ranch, 4th Filing and this plat may be able 

UDC-21-00095
June 7, 2021
to meet the collective open space required. A demonstration of how both plats combined meet 
the combined requirement, and an agreement between the different property representatives 
would be necessary to fulfill the open space requirement.   
 
Pursuant to UCD 4.4.2.b.4, the discretion of the City the applicant may make a contribution to 
the City in lieu of park land donation.  Staff recommends that with final plat submittal the 
applicant provide acceptable documentation indicating the value of lands that would have been 
dedicated. Staff believes that an in-lieu contribution to the open space development is 
acceptable for this development.   
 
A natural area, park, or other dedication may also be a possibility to provide the required open 
space. 
 
Required Improvements: 
This proposed subdivision includes a layout to provide required utilities to the properties within 
this development; however, additional easements may be required.  
 
New traffic control improvements may be necessary as this area of the community develops, 
and this portion of the overall development in the area should contribute to such 
improvements. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.  
  
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
There is no phasing proposed regarding the subdivision of the subject property. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
The applicant submitted a drainage worksheet and a Traffic Impact Study. No other studies were 
provided with the Preliminary Plat application. 
 
Drainage: 
The review of the drainage worksheet submitted with the preliminary plat identified that a 
drainage study is required for this project. No drainage study has been submitted. 
 
Traffic: 
After review, the traffic analysis submitted with this application was deemed deficient by the 
. Multiple corrections or adjustments were requested. An updated traffic 
impact study has not been submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   

UDC-21-00095
June 7, 2021
6)
Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The lots on this plat meet required lot standards in the proposed zone districts; however, type 
of development may be limited based on the layout shown.  
 
The following lots are short in area or frontage for the required minimums for detached (single-
family) development in the LR zone: Lots 3, 4, 8, 15, and 28. Unless adjusted, these lots may be 
restricted to duplex development. 
 
Lot 3 is the only lot short in area, and is less than 500sqft short  the adjacent Lot 4 is more than 
1,600sqft over the minimum and a minor adjustment may be able to make both lots over the 
standard.  
 
The UDC allows lot frontage to be calculated at the front setback line instead of the front lot line 
(for cul-de-sacs or similar curved rights-of-way). With this allowance, Lots 3, 4, 8, and 28 may 
meet frontage minimums; and, 
meet minimums for detached (single-family) development.   
 
Lots 3 and Lots 7-24 on Block 3 are short of the minimum area requirements for detached 
(single-family) development in the MR zone, but meet the standards for duplexes. 
 
The LR zone is intended to develop with a mix of lower-density, suburban residential 
development. The MR zone is intended to develop with a mix of residential dwelling types and 
densities. The CB zone is intended for Retail, Employment, Service and Civic uses that may serve 
a number of residential neighborhoods. The general development patterns proposed meet the 
intent and applicability of their respective zone districts.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
All proposed lots are proposed to be buildable. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
Surrounding properties to the north and east are largely already developed with rural residential 
uses in the County. Property to the south is largely developed with a mix of residential, 
commercial, and light-industrial style uses. The property to the west is undeveloped, but has 
had an approved preliminary plat with a mix of commercial and residential uses. Directly 
adjacent to the proposed development to the south and west are major roadways.  
 

UDC-21-00095
June 7, 2021
The plat does not propose any connections to the north or east, but a significant topographic 
change and previous development limits the ability to make any effective connections. 
 
Lots proposed by this plat are smaller in size when compared to existing development patterns; 
but most of the surrounding development is outside of City limits and the future land uses of 
this area anticipate future urban-style development. Due to the intersection of major roadways, 
and nearby development to the south, the placement of commercial lots is appropriate. 
 
The Comprehensive Plan designates most of the area inside and around the plat boundary as 
Mixed-use Commercial and Urban Transition Residential. The development pattern proposed by 
this plat is appropriate for this mix of designations. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
The parent lots within the original plat for Meadowlark Estates do not meet present septic 
system size requirements but were approved in conformance with the standards at the time of 
separation.  Staff believes that with this development being connected to City water and sewer, 
the application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare.  
 
Access location and spacing is a public safety concern which is why the standards of Article 4 
exist. The plat proposes potentially unsafe intersections and accesses onto Dell Range. For 
safety reasons, the proposed access points may result in limited turning movements. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this Preliminary Plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
East Dell Range Boulevard / US 30 Corridor Study 
 
Whitney Road Corridor Plan 
 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
Both the East Dell Range Boulevard / US 30 Corridor Study and the Whitney Road Corridor Plan 
recommend the expansion of Dell Range. The preferred option for the improvement of the Dell Range 
and Whitney intersection is a roundabout; however, a traffic signal-controlled intersection was also put 
forward as an option. The proposed plat anticipates a traffic signal; however, either option needs to 
dedicate appropriate space for improvements at the intersection. These plans also anticipate the 
development of this area with urban-style uses.   

UDC-21-00095
June 7, 2021
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
The applicant responded to initial Staff comments and have submitted revisions and clarifications.  
Comments and concerns that have been addressed between staff report publications have been 
removed from this report. Through the review process of the revised submittal , comments were 
received regarding the following issues: 
1. Lack of a Drainage Study 
2. Traffic Study deficiencies 
3. Dedicating a triangle of right-of-way at the Dell Range and Whitney Road intersection (to allow 
for intersection improvements) 
4. Concern about the Woodhouse Dr intersection
intersection and potential movement restrictions 
5. Sidewalks along Whitney Road 
6. Porter right-of-way width 
7. Lack of required Open Space 
8. Block sizes and double-fronted lots 
9. Finch Drive width 
10. Effect of nearby septic systems 
11. Lack of utility easements 
 
At the time of staff report publication, these items were still of concern and staff has recommended 
conditions to address these concerns at final platting.  
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
This application meets review criteria 4, 6, 7, 8, 9, and 10 with the revisions submitted. Conditioning the 
Preliminary Plat may allow the plat to meet review criteria 1, 2, 3, and 5. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat 
 (Staff 
Recommendation). 
3. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the plat does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, findings 
for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the preliminary plat until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved.  
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
conditions, or 
modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for 
approval or denial. 
 
 
 
 
 
 

UDC-21-00095
June 7, 2021
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval with the following conditions: 
1. Concurrent with submittal of the final plat, the applicant shall: 
a. Provide a drainage study 
b. Provide an updated traffic study 
c. Revise the plat as follows: 
i. Make Porter Dr. 
-of-way. 
ii. Provide a street section for Finch Dr. satisfactory for Engineering,  
iii. Provide necessary utility easements. 
iv. Make revisions (as necessary) to accommodate drainage and open space  
2. Prior to approval of the final plat the applicant shall:  
a. Enter into a development agreement for public safety fees. Public safety fees shall be 
based on a January 2021 fee of $756 per unit and shall be adjusted for inflation utilizing 
the construction cost index for twenty (20) cities, as issued by the current Engineering 
News Record to the date of final plat filing, and this condition shall be subject to 
modification by the Governing Body at the time of their consideration. 
b. Obtain a Subdivision Standard Waiver for alternative street design for construction of 
Finch Drive (or widen to a standard street right-of-way width).  
c. Obtain a Subdivision Standard Waiver for relief from the sidewalk construction 
requirements on the east side of Whitney Road north of proposed Porter Drive.  
d. Enter into a development agreement regarding improvements to Dell Range Boulevard 
mprovements.  
e. Provide required open space through either 
i. dedication on this plat; OR 
ii. documented agreement(s) with the neighboring Whitney Ranch, 4th Filing plat 
iii. acceptable cash-in lieu pursuant to UDC 4.2.2.b.4.  
3. In the event the applicant desires to provide cash in lieu instead of dedicated open space, the 
applicant shall provide acceptable documentation indicating the value of land(s) that would 
have been dedicated.

STAFF REPORT 
 
FILE: 
UDC-21-00168: The View Preliminary Plat  
REQUEST: 
A Preliminary Plat of 1.3 acres proposing creating 9 lots 
LOCATION: 
Southwest of the intersection of Parkhill, Braehill, and Panorama  
APPLICANT: 
TL Provision, LLC 
AGENT: 
AVI, PC  
OWNER: 
Darold Stephen Melchior, Et Ux 
ZONING: 
County AR Agricultural Residential 
PURPOSE: 
A preliminary plat proposing 9 lots on a 1.3 acre tract of land for future 
residential development  
PREPARED BY: 
Seth Lloyd, Planner 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

UDC-21-00168
June 7, 2021
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to recommend an exception for block size and approve the The View Preliminary Plat subject to 
approval. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval  
 
UDC Article 3 Impact Studies 
 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
East Dell Range Boulevard / US 30 Corridor Study 
 
Whitney Road Corridor Plan 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for a 1.3 acre tract of land to create 9 lots for future 
residential development. The County Assessor presently shows the tract of land as part of three ownership 
parcels.  The land is currently developed with one rural-residential home.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
A majority of the property is currently zoned County AR Agricultural Residential, but has recently 
submitted an application to be annexed and zoned with the City zone of MR Medium-density Residential 
The remainder of the property is currently within City limits and zoned MR Medium-density Residential.

UDC-21-00168
June 7, 2021
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the preliminary plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, the subject land has been designated as 
Urban Residential and is within the Urban Service Boundary. Surrounding areas are also 
designated Urban Residential.  The Urban Residential designation encourages a range of 
residential uses. The optimal City zoning is any of the residential zones, including the MR zone. 
The plat proposes meeting the MR zoning standards, which would be in conformance with the 
future land use map.  
 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Foundation 7 of PlanCheyenne support the use of existing infrastructure and timely provision of 
services (including emergency and education services). Developments on the edge of the 
community can create issues with providing services in an effective and timely fashion. 
Development of this area of the community creates the need to improve street and emergency 
service infrastructure. Staff recommends that preliminary plats expanding the response area 
include conditions for agreements with the City regarding impacts to Cheyenne Fire Rescue and 
the design and construction of roadway improvements. 
 
Other principles of PlanCheyenne encourage tying neighborhoods together and a compatible 
development pattern between neighborhoods. Due to topographic constraints and previous 
development, the ability for the proposed subdivision to tie into the land to the north and east 
is limited. While the subdivision to the north is largely rural, the areas to the south and east are 
urban in nature and are zoned MR. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed plat is adjacent to an existing rural development to the north, and near existing 
urban residential development to the south and east. The plat proposes developing land near 
existing rights-of-way. Due to topography and previous development patterns, there is no real 
opportunity to tie into the neighborhood to the southwest.  
 
There may be opportunity for properties to the north to redevelop into a more urban residential 
style, and utilities would need to be extended to such development. Some utilities already are 
extended northward. Extending sanitary sewer may assist redevelopment, but sanitary sewer 
may need to go a different direction due to this location being a topographic high point.   
 

UDC-21-00168
June 7, 2021
-
en buildings, fences may be built to the property line. A less-than-
fences would make the triangular-shaped western portions of Lot 1 difficult to access and 
maintain. Staff recommends that the western, triangular-shaped portions of Lot 1 be 
consolidated into the duplex lots. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.  
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Transportation Network: 
The transportation network in this area of the community is largely previously developed and is 
topographically constrained. There is limited opportunity for this plat to make any meaningful 
connections to the street network. The plat does not propose any dedication of right-of-way. 
The block this proposed plat is part of is too large by UDC standards, but due to topographic 
constraints and previous development, this block qualifies for an exception. 
 
Community Facility Fees: 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. The 
required fees for the 0.7 acres of non-exempt land in this plat are as follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$245 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
 
$245 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$490.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat. However, as this land was previously platted in the 
County after the adoption of the UDC, fees are due upon annexation. These required fees 
would be paid with the annexation instead of with this platting action.  
 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Commercial Enhancement Fee 
$1,100 per acre 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
 
Open Space: 
Open space is required to be provided with this subdivision. For low-density residential 
development, open space is required at 750 square feet per dwelling unit or 8% of the gross plat 
area (whichever is less).  
 
This plat proposes a large common lot which would likely be used for access, detention, and 
open space. While the area used for access should not count toward the open space 
requirement, the proposed lot is over 50% of the entire development and would likely still more 
than satisfy the open space requirement even with access areas subtracted out. 
 
Required Improvements: 
This proposed subdivision includes a layout to provide required utilities to the properties within 
this development; however, additional easements may be required.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with an approved exception.  

UDC-21-00168
June 7, 2021
4)
Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
There is no phasing proposed regarding the subdivision of the subject property. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
The applicant submitted a drainage worksheet, a drainage plan and a Transportation Worksheet 
with the initial submittal. No other studies were provided with the Preliminary Plat application. 
A revised Drainage Plan was submitted based on Staff comments. 
 
Drainage: 
The review of the revised drainage documents submitted with the preliminary plat identified 
issues with some of the calculations provided. A revision (or justification) is required. Concerns 
were also raised about the current and potential erosion of the hillside. 
 
Traffic: 
After review, the traffic analysis submitted with this application was deemed sufficient. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.  
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
Lot 1 is proposed as an open space or access lot and is not proposed to be buildable. The 
remaining 8 lots are proposed as duplex lots in the MR zone. All 8 of these lots meet minimum 
area requirements, but 7 do not meet requirements for frontage at the front lot line. The 
 If the gaps created by Lot 1 were consolidated, these lots 
would be closer to the required minimum at the front lot line. 
 
The UDC does allow lot frontage to be calculated at the front building line (typically the front 
setback line) instead of the front lot line (for cul-de-sacs or similar curved rights-of-way). If this 
allowance is applied to these lots, each lot would meet required frontage minimums.   
 
Buildable lots are intended to front on public rights of way. The lots contained within this 
development do not front the public right-of-way, but instead front upon an open space / access 
parcel. However, there is nothing intervening between the right-of-way and the proposed 
duplex lots, so the duplex lot lines could be extended to the Panorama right-of-way, dissolving 
Lot 1 entirely, and instead creating a
-
 of 

UDC-21-00168
June 7, 2021
each longer lot. Such a configuration would be able to more precisely meet the intent of code 
requirements, but may be more difficult for clarity of ownership and maintenance. The layout 
presented is more functional and substantively meets the frontage requirements. These are not 
row of 
houses. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
Lot 1 is proposed to be owned by an undivided interest of the owners of the other 8 lots, and an 
HOA is proposed to maintain this unbuildable lot. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
The land immediately adjacent to the north and northeast are dedicated rights-of-way. The land 
to the south and west has significant topographic change. The land to the east is developed with 
single-family homes. All of the surrounding land is designated Urban Residential by the future 
land use map in Plan Cheyenne, and surrounding existing zoning is AR in the County and MR in 
the City. The existing development patterns, zoning, and future land use designation all promote 
residential development.  
 
While duplex-style development does not currently exist in the neighborhood, urban-scale 
residential does. The density allowed by the existing City zoning and the type of development 
anticipated by the future land use map designation imply that this style of proposed 
development is compatible with the neighborhood, although not identical to existing 
on surrounding property. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
The area contained within this plat is part of a significant hillside. There is concern about the 
potential erosion of the hillside due to the development. Further documentation and analysis of 
the 
hillside is required to determine how to mitigate potential 
erosion.  
 
Staff is satisfied this application would not otherwise unduly influence the public health, safety, 
and welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.   
 

UDC-21-00168
June 7, 2021
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this Preliminary Plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Lack of easements 
2. The configuration of Lot 1  specifically in relation to the narrow gaps 
3. Public Safety Fees 
4. Extension of BOPU facilities 
5. Construction of public improvements 
6. Drainage plan issues 
7. Potential erosion issues 
8. Block size (and exception) 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
This application meets review criteria 3, 4, 6, 7, 8, and 10 (with an exception). Conditioning the 
Preliminary Plat may allow the plat to meet review criteria 1, 2, 5, and 9. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat 
 (Staff 
Recommendation). 
3. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the plat does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, findings 
for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the preliminary plat until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2021-06-01_TotalExtraction_63441447.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001

MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director 
Subject: Agreement for public safety fees for Saddle Ridge 16th Filing final plat.   
Date: 
May 24, 2021  
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is an agreement for public safety fees associated with the Saddle Ridge 16th 
Filing final plat.  The final plat is a separate item on this agenda.  
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral: May 24, 2021 
• 
Public Service Committee: June 8, 2021 
• 
Final consideration: June 14, 2021 
 
On May 3, 2021, the Planning Commission approved a preliminary plat and recommended approval of 
an expedited plat subject to specific conditions (as noted on the corresponding plat item also on this 
agenda).  Condition 1.c. was as follows: 
 
“Prior to approval of the final plat, the applicant shall: Enter into a development agreement for 
public safety fees. Public safety fees shall be based on a January 2021 fee of $756 per unit and 
shall be adjusted for inflation utilizing the construction cost index for twenty (20) cities, as 
issued by the current Engineering News Record to the date of final plat filing, and this condition 
shall be subject to modification by the Governing Body at the time of their consideration.”  
 
This agreement satisfies condition 1.c. For the purpose of establishing the fee staff utilized a fee of $756 
per unit that was deemed acceptable by the Governing Body in their recent approval of the Whitney 
Range 3rd Filing Development agreement. This fee was adjusted for inflation utilizing the construction 
cost index for twenty (20) cities, as issued by the current Engineering News Record to the date of final 
plat filing.  The inflation-adjusted fee totals $793.89 per dwelling unit. The total fees for this 
development will be $20,641.14 which will be placed in the Development Impact Fund earmarked 
for the purpose of enhancing the City’s fire rescue infrastructure. 
 
Staff Recommendation: 
 
Staff recommends the Governing Body approve the agreement as it satisfies preliminary plat condition 
1.c.   
 
Attachments: 
1. Agreement for Public Safety Fees 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department

appropriate for the Development. 
  
NOW, THEREFORE, in consideration of the promises of the parties hereto and other 
good and valuable consideration, the receipt and adequacy of which are hereby acknowledged, 
the Parties agree as follows:  
 
1. 
Recitals Incorporated.  The foregoing recitals are incorporated herein by reference 
into this Agreement as though set forth at length. 
 
2. 
The Parties agree to the following public safety fees regarding public safety 
improvements: 
 
A. Public safety fees total forty-eight thousand four hundred and twenty-seven 
dollars and seven cents ($20,641.05). 
 
B. Public safety fees shall be collected by the City at the time of initial building 
permit application within Saddle Ridge 16th Filing final plat within the 
Development.  Public safety fees shall be based on subdivision plat 
assumptions regarding number of units as provided in subsection A above. 
 
C. Public safety fees shall be deposited into the Development Impact Fund and 
segregated for the purpose of public safety and infrastructure. 
 
D. The Developer may, at any time, request a report of the account activity in 
the Development Impact Fund and the City Treasurer shall deliver said 
report within thirty (30) days. 
 
E. If the City implements a City-wide public safety fee requirement for 
residential development with fees due at the time of building permit 
application, lots within the Development shall be exempt from fee 
requirements. Any funds paid into the Development Impact Fund in excess 
of the adopted fee schedule shall be refunded to the Developer within sixty 
(60) days of adoption of said public safety fee requirement.  Shall the City 
enter into a subsequent development agreement requiring a lessor per unit 
fee, the difference of funds paid into the Development Impact Fund in 
excess of the subsequent development agreement per unit fee shall be 
refunded to the Developer within sixty (60) days of adoption of said public 
safety fee requirement. If the City does not implement a City-wide public 

Page 3 of 5 
 
safety fee requirement for residential development within twenty-four (24) 
months of this Agreement, public safety fees paid shall be refunded to the 
Developer upon request by the Developer.  Any request for a refund in 
whole or in part shall be made by the Developer and shall occur within sixty 
(60) days of an event triggering refund. 
 
3. 
Binding Effect. This Agreement shall run with the Property for a period of fifteen 
(15) years and shall commence after the final approval of all phases of the 
Development by the City. This Agreement shall also be binding upon and inure to 
the benefit of the parties hereto, their respective personal representatives, heirs, 
successors, grantees and assigns. Assignment of interest within the meaning of this 
paragraph shall specifically include, but not be limited to, a conveyance or 
assignment of any portion of the Developer’s legal or equitable interest in the 
Property, as well as any assignment of the Developer’s rights to develop the 
Property under the terms and conditions of this Agreement. This Agreement shall 
be recorded against all lots within future phases of the Development. 
 
4. 
Relationship of Parties.  Nothing contained in this Agreement shall be deemed or 
construed, either by the parties hereto or by any third party, to create the 
relationship of principal and agent or create any partnership, joint venture or other 
association between the City and the Developer.   
 
5. 
Counterparts. This Agreement may be executed in multiple counterparts, each of 
which shall be deemed an original, but all of which, together, shall constitute one 
and the same instrument. 
 
6. 
Applicable Law.  This Agreement shall be governed by and construed and enforced 
in accordance with the laws of the State of Wyoming. 
 
7. 
Governmental Immunity. The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and 
employees, does not waive its governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, 
except to the extent necessary for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or 
enforce the written terms of the Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains 
all immunities and defenses available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity 
pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. 
Designations of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and similar provisions 
should not be constructed as a waiver of governmental immunity. The parties agree that 
any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not be strictly construed, either against or for 
either party, except that any ambiguity as to governmental or sovereign immunity shall 
be construed in favor of governmental immunity. 
 
8. 
Entire Agreement.  This Agreement (including the Exhibits attached hereto) 
contains the entire agreement between the parties with respect to the subject matter 
hereof and supersedes all prior and contemporaneous representations, statements, 
understandings, negotiations and agreements, oral or written, between the parties, 
if any, with respect thereto. 
 
 
 

Page 4 of 5 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties hereto have executed this Agreement on the day 
and year first written above. 
 
 
CITY: 
 
City of Cheyenne, a Municipal Corporation 
 
 
_________________________________ 
By:  Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
(SEAL) 
 
ATTEST: 
 
_________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
 
 
DEVELOPER: 
 
WJE LLC, a Limited Liability Corporation 
 
 
_________________________________ 
By:  John M. Edwards, Manager
